उक्षान्नाय वशान्नायेत्याग्नीध्रो यजति सोमपृष्टाय वेधस इतींद्रो वै वेधास्तदैंद्रं रूपं तेनेंद्रं प्रीणाति । (*ukṣānnāya vaśānnāyetyāgnīdhro yajati somapṛṣṭāya vedhasa itīṃdro vai vedhāstadaiṃdraṃ rūpaṃ teneṃdraṃ prīṇāti |*)

The Ṛtvij called Agnīdhra recites and offers the yājyā mantra उक्षान्नाय वशान्नाय (*ukṣānnāya vaśānnāya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-43-11). In the second pāda of this mantra the word वेधसे (*vedhase*) is recited, a form of the word Vedhas. The word Vedhas means one who ordains the arrangement of the world. Because he ordains the arrangement of the world, Indra is denoted by the word Vedhas. Therefore this Ṛk is pleasing to Indra.

प्रातर्यावभिरागतं देवेभिर्जेन्यावसू । इंद्राग्नी सोमपीतॆय इति स्वयं समृद्धाच्छावाकस्य । (*prātaryāvabhirāgataṃ devebhirjenyāvasū | iṃdrāgnī somapītèya iti svayaṃ samṛddhācchāvākasya |*)

The Ṛtvij called Acchāvāka recites and offers as a yājyā mantra the Ṛk प्रातर्यावभिरागतं (*prātaryāvabhirāgataṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-38-7). The main purport of this Ṛk is: O deities Indra and Agni, you are those who have conquered the enemies and obtained the riches they held. Such as you are, come here for the drinking of Soma, together with the deities who come at dawn to the place of sacrifice for the Prātaḥsavana. In this Ṛk the expression इंद्राग्नी (*iṃdrāgnī*) is used and the two deities Agni and Indra are both indicated; yet, since the word Indra is used directly, this Ṛk is pleasing to Indra. This is the purport.

एतमु हैता ऐंद्रो भवंति यन्नानादेवत्यास्तेनान्या देवताः प्रीणाति । यदु गायत्र्यस्तेनाग्नेय्यः एतदु हैताभिस्त्रयमुपाप्नोति ॥ ( (*etamu haitā aiṃdro bhavaṃti yannānādevatyāstenānyā devatāḥ prīṇāti | yadu gāyatryastenāgneyyaḥ etadu haitābhistrayamupāpnoti ‖ (*)

(Ai. Brā. 28-1)

In this way the Indra-nature of the yājyā mantras recited by the seven Ṛtvijs has been set forth. Besides, the deities spoken of in these Ṛks, namely the Mitrāvaruṇas, the Maruts, Agni, Vedhas and Indrāgnī, have also been pleased. Since all these Ṛks are in the Gāyatrī metre, they are connected with Agni. How? Since Agni and Gāyatrī were both born from the mouth of Prajāpati, all the Ṛks in the Gāyatrī metre have an indirect connection with Agni. Therefore in these yājyā mantras the praise of various deities

### Printed p. 59

and the prosperity of Indra's deityship (a description relating to Indra), and the connection with Agni through the Gāyatrī metre, these three kinds of connection have been explained.

Here the explanation of the Prātaḥsavana ends.

**The Explanation of the Mādhyandina-savana**

In the Mādhyandina-savana, as soon as the Unnīyamāna sūkta has been directed to be recited by the Praiṣa mantra, the Maitrāvaruṇa recites the sūkta असावि देवं गोऋजीकमंधः (*asāvi devaṃ goṛjīkamaṃdhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ., the 21st sūkta of Maṇḍala 7).

असावि देवं गोऋजीकमंध इति मध्यंदिन उन्नीयमानेभ्योऽन्वाह वृषण्वतीः पीतवतीः सुतवतीर्मद्वतीः रूपसमृद्धाः । (*asāvi devaṃ goṛjīkamaṃdha iti madhyaṃdina unnīyamānebhyo 'nvāha vṛṣaṇvatīḥ pītavatīḥ sutavatīrmadvatīḥ rūpasamṛddhāḥ |*)

This sūkta, as stated before, has ten Ṛks. In the Ṛks of this sūkta there are the words vṛṣa, pīta, suta and mada, which are used for the purpose of drinking Soma. How? In the second Ṛk of this sūkta the word वृषणो नृषाच (*vṛṣaṇo nṛṣāca*), that is, the word vṛṣaṇa, is recited. In the third Ṛk, रथ्यो न धेना (*rathyo na dhenā*), the word dhenā is used. Since this word is derived from the root धेट् पाने (*dheṭ pāne*), "to drink", the use of this word indicates the sense of the word pīta. The word असावि (*asāvi*) at the very beginning of the sūkta is derived from the root su, "to press", and indicates the sense of the word suta; and the word मदेषु (*madeṣu*) in अंधसो मदेषु (*aṃdhaso madeṣu*) at the end of the first Ṛk indicates the state of mada. Therefore this sūkta, containing the words vṛṣa, pīta, suta and mada, which belong to Soma-drinking, and having a meaning suited to the present occasion, thus has richness of form.

ऐंद्रीरन्वाहैंद्रो वै यज्ञस्त्रिष्टुभोऽन्वाह त्रैष्टुभं वै माध्यंदिनं सवनं । (*aiṃdrīranvāhaiṃdro vai yajñastriṣṭubho 'nvāha traiṣṭubhaṃ vai mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ |*)

He now examines the Ṛks of this sūkta, and their metre and deities. According to the Śruti statement ऐंद्रो वै यज्ञः (*aiṃdro vai yajñaḥ*), the sacrifice must be of the nature of Indra. Accordingly, since the word Indra is used in the second pāda of the first Ṛk, Indra-nature has been stated for this sūkta. In the other Ṛks too Indra-nature is indicated in the same way. According to the rule that at the Mādhyandina-savana mantras in the Triṣṭubh metre are to be recited, it is well known that this sūkta consists of Ṛks in the Triṣṭubh metre.

### Printed p. 60

तदाहुर्यत्तृतीयसवनस्यॆव रूपं मद्वदथ कस्मान्मध्यंदिने मद्वतीरनु चाह यजंति चाभिरिति । (*tadāhuryattṛtīyasavanasyèva rūpaṃ madvadatha kasmānmadhyaṃdine madvatīranu cāha yajaṃti cābhiriti |*)

In this sūkta, with the word मदेषु (*madeṣu*), a matter connected with the intoxication produced by drinking Soma is stated. But at the third savana, the last savana, performed at the end of the day, joy arises in all the deities after they have drunk Soma, and so it is natural that the word mada should be used then. Is it not unsuitable that, instead of that, Ṛks in which the word mada is used should be recited in the Mādhyandina-savana itself? This seems strange. To this he says:

माद्यंतीव वै मध्यंदिने देवताः समेव तृतीयसवने मादयंते तस्मान्मध्यंदिने मद्वतीरनु चाह यजंति चाभिः । (*mādyaṃtīva vai madhyaṃdine devatāḥ sameva tṛtīyasavane mādayaṃte tasmānmadhyaṃdine madvatīranu cāha yajaṃti cābhiḥ |*)

He gives the answer to this. Even at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana some chief deities become joyful after drinking Soma. At the third savana the other deities too, who did not drink Soma at the Mādhyandina-savana, become joyful after drinking Soma. Therefore it is fitting that the Maitrāvaruṇa should recite, at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana, Ṛks in which the word mada is used. In the same way the seven Ṛtvijs, beginning with the Hotṛ, also make their offerings at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana with yājyā mantras in which the word mada is used.

ते वै खलु सर्व एव माध्यंदिने प्रस्थितानां प्रत्यक्षाद्यंद्रीभिर्यजंति अभितृण्णवतीभिरेके । (*te vai khalu sarva eva mādhyaṃdine prasthitānāṃ pratyakṣādyaṃdrībhiryajaṃti abhitṛṇṇavatībhireke |*)

He now explains the nature of the prasthita yājyā mantras with which the Ṛtvijs beginning with the Hotṛ make their offerings. In the prasthita yājyā mantras that the seven Ṛtvijs, beginning with the Hotṛ, use for the offering in this Mādhyandina-savana, the word Indra ought to be used directly. But only in the yājyā mantras recited by the three Ṛtvijs, the Hotṛ, the Maitrāvaruṇa and the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsī, are there words indicating Indra-nature, and also the word अभितृण्ण (*abhitṛṇṇa*), which indicates drinking. The word अभितृण्ण (*abhitṛṇṇa*) is derived from the root तृदि (*tṛdi*) joined with the prefix अभि (*abhi*) and indicates the sense of drinking.

पिबा सोममभि यमुग्र तर्द इति होता यजति । (*pibā somamabhi yamugra tarda iti hotā yajati |*)

He explains the yājyā mantras that the seven Ṛtvijs, beginning with the Hotṛ, recite one by one. First the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk पिबा सोममभि यमुग्र तर्दः (*pibā somamabhi yamugra tardaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-17-1). In this Ṛk the words अभि (*abhi*) and तर्द (*tarda*) together indicate the word अभितृण्ण (*abhitṛṇṇa*).

स ईं पाहि य ऋजीषी तरुत्र इति मैत्रावरुणो यजति । (*sa īṃ pāhi ya ṛjīṣī tarutra iti maitrāvaruṇo yajati |*)

### Printed p. 61

The Ṛtvij called Maitrāvaruṇa recites the Ṛk स ईं पाहि य ऋजीषी तरुत्रः (*sa īṃ pāhi ya ṛjīṣī tarutraḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-17-2). In the fourth pāda of this Ṛk the word अभितृंधि (*abhitṛṃdhi*) indicates the sense of the word अभितृण्ण (*abhitṛṇṇa*).

एवा पाहि प्रत्नथा मंदतु त्वेति ब्राह्मणाच्छंसी यजति । (*evā pāhi pratnathā maṃdatu tveti brāhmaṇācchaṃsī yajati |*)

The Ṛtvij called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsī recites the Ṛk एवा पाहि प्रत्नथा मंदतु त्वा (*evā pāhi pratnathā maṃdatu tvā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-17-3). In the fourth pāda of this Ṛk the word अभितृंधि (*abhitṛṃdhi*) is used, in अभिगा इंद्र तृंधि (*abhigā iṃdra tṛṃdhi*). In this way, in the yājyā mantras recited by the three Ṛtvijs, the Hotṛ, the Maitrāvaruṇa and the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsī, the words Indra and abhitṛṇṇa (drinking) are found used.

अर्वाजेहि सोमकामं त्वाहुरिति पोता यजति तवायं सोमस्वमेह्यर्वाजति नेष्टा यजति इंद्राय सोमाः प्रदिवो विदाना इत्यच्छावाको यजति आपूर्णो अस्य कलशः स्वाहेत्याग्निध्रो यजति । (*arvājehi somakāmaṃ tvāhuriti potā yajati tavāyaṃ somasvamehyarvājati neṣṭā yajati iṃdrāya somāḥ pradivo vidānā ityacchāvāko yajati āpūrṇo asya kalaśaḥ svāhetyāgnidhro yajati |*)

He now indicates the yājyā mantras recited by the remaining four Ṛtvijs. The Ṛtvij called Potṛ recites the Ṛk आर्वाजेहि सोमकामं त्वाहुः (*ārvājehi somakāmaṃ tvāhuḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-104-9); the Ṛtvij called Neṣṭṛ the Ṛk तवायं सोमस्तमेह्यर्वाज् (*tavāyaṃ somastamehyarvāj*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-35-6) **NOTE**; the Ṛtvij called Acchāvāka the Ṛk इंद्राय सोमाः प्रदिवो विदानाः (*iṃdrāya somāḥ pradivo vidānāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-36-2); and the Ṛtvij called Āgnīdhra **NOTE** the Ṛk आपूर्णो अस्य कलशः स्वाहा (*āpūrṇo asya kalaśaḥ svāhā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-32-15), each as his own yājyā mantra.

तासामेता अभितृण्णवत्यो भवंतींद्रो वै प्रातःसवने न व्यजयत स एताभिरेव माध्यंदिनं सवनमभ्यतृणद्यदभ्यतृणत्तस्मादेता अभितृण्णवत्यो भवंति । (*tāsāmetā abhitṛṇṇavatyo bhavaṃtīṃdro vai prātaḥsavane na vyajayata sa etābhireva mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamabhyatṛṇadyadabhyatṛṇattasmādetā abhitṛṇṇavatyo bhavaṃti |*)

Of the seven yājyā mantras described above, the first three Ṛks are well known as the abhitṛṇṇavatī mantras. They are so named because in these Ṛks there is a word derived from the root तृदि (*tṛdi*) preceded by the prefix अभि (*abhi*). Since the root तृदि (*tṛdi*) has the sense of injuring, killing, the words abhitardana or abhitṛṇṇa indicate Indra's power to subdue enemies. How? Formerly, when Indra drank Soma at the Prātaḥsavana and set out to conquer the enemies, victory did not come to him. But at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana the Ṛtvijs recited the Ṛks beginning पिबा सोमं (*pibā somaṃ*), in which the word abhitarda (abhitṛṇṇa), indicating the subduing of enemies, is used, and so increased Indra's strength. And Indra too, at the Mādhyandina-savana, drank Soma abundantly and from the joy he derived from it

### Printed p. 62

became able to defeat the enemies. In this way, since the strength of Indra has been made firmly bound by the word abhitarda in the first three Ṛks beginning पिबा सोमं (*pibā somaṃ*), the name abhitṛṇṇavatī Ṛks for these three Ṛks is appropriate to their meaning.

Here the explanation of the Mādhyandina-savana ends.

**The Explanation of the Third Savana**

As stated before, at the beginning of the third savana the Ṛtvij called Maitrāvaruṇa recites the Unnīyamāna sūkta.

इहोप यात शवसो नपात इति तृतीयसवन उन्नीयमानेभ्योऽन्वाह वृषण्वतीः पीतवतीः सुतवतीर्मद्वती रूपसमृद्धास्ता ऐंद्रार्भव्यो भवंति। (*ihopa yāta śavaso napāta iti tṛtīyasavana unnīyamānebhyo 'nvāha vṛṣaṇvatīḥ pītavatīḥ sutavatīrmadvatī rūpasamṛddhāstā aiṃdrārbhavyo bhavaṃti|*)

At the beginning of the third savana, as soon as the Adhvaryu has directed the Maitrāvaruṇa by the Praiṣa mantra to recite the Unnīyamāna sūkta, the Maitrāvaruṇa recites the sūkta of nine Ṛks इहोप यात शवसो नपात (*ihopa yāta śavaso napāta*) (Ṛ. Saṃ., the 35th sūkta of Maṇḍala 4). In the Ṛks of this sūkta the words vṛṣa, pīta, suta and mada are used. How? In the fourth pāda of the 6th Ṛk of this sūkta it says आ तक्षत वृषणो मंदसानाः (*ā takṣata vṛṣaṇo maṃdasānāḥ*), so the word vṛṣaṇa is there; in the second pāda of the 2nd Ṛk it says सुषुतस्य पीतिः (*suṣutasya pītiḥ*), so the words suta and pīta are there; and in the fourth pāda of the first Ṛk, इंद्रमनु वो मदासः (*iṃdramanu vo madāsaḥ*), the word mada is there. Therefore this sūkta has richness of form. The deities of the Ṛks of this sūkta are Indra and the Ṛbhus, the sons of Sudhanvan. Although the Anukramaṇikā names the Ṛbhus as the deity of this sūkta, there is no reason for doubt that Indra too is a deity of this sūkta, since in the first Ṛk, सौधन्वना ऋभवो............गमत्त्विंद्रमनु वो मदासः (*saudhanvanā ṛbhavo............gamattviṃdramanu vo madāsaḥ*), and in the 7th Ṛk, समृभुभिः पिबस्व..................सखीयान् इंद्र (*samṛbhubhiḥ pibasva..................sakhīyān iṃdra*), it is prayed that Soma be drunk together with the Ṛbhus, addressing Indra.

तदाहुर्यन्नार्भवीषु स्तुवतेऽथ कस्मादार्भवः पवमान इत्याचक्षत इति । (*tadāhuryannārbhavīṣu stuvate 'tha kasmādārbhavaḥ pavamāna ityācakṣata iti |*)

Is not the presence of a connection with the Ṛbhus at the time of the third savana open to objection? And the Sāma singers at the sacrifice do not, at the third savana, combine Ṛks having the Ṛbhus as their deity and praise the Ṛbhus with the Pavamāna stotra; they praise with mantras that have other deities than the Ṛbhus, beginning स्वादिष्टया मदिष्टया (*svādiṣṭayā madiṣṭayā*). Therefore how the Pavamāna stotra comes to have the Ārbhava nature, that is, a connection with the Ṛbhus, is also strange. To this he gives the answer.

### Printed p. 63

प्रजापतिर्वै पित ऋभून्मर्त्यान्सतोऽमर्त्यान्पृत्वा तृतीयसवन आभजत्तस्मान्नार्भवीषु स्तुवतेऽथार्भवः पवमान इत्याचक्षते । (*prajāpatirvai pita ṛbhūnmartyānsato 'martyānpṛtvā tṛtīyasavana ābhajattasmānnārbhavīṣu stuvate 'thārbhavaḥ pavamāna ityācakṣate |*)

Why is the Pavamāna stotra recited at the time of the third savana called Ārbhava (accompanied by the Ṛbhus)? The Ṛks of this stotra are not mantras having the Ṛbhus as their deity. To this there is an ancient story. Formerly Prajāpati, the father of all, made the Ṛbhus, who had been human, into deathless deities (we have explained this matter earlier in the Ṛbhu sūktas), and granted them the right to drink Soma at the third savana. Therefore the Ṛbhus are entitled to a share of Soma at the third savana only, and for this reason it is said here आर्भवं शंसति (*ārbhavaṃ śaṃsati*) (the Ṛbhus are praised). Since this is so, and since the Ṛbhus are not deities in the stotras, the Ṛtvijs who sing the sāmans, the Udgātṛ and the others, do not praise the Ṛbhus in the Pavamāna stotras; yet the Pavamāna sūkta that praises another deity (Pavamāna Soma) is called the Ārbhava Pavamāna sūkta.

अथाह यद्यथाच्छंदसं पूर्वयोः सवनयोरन्वाह गायत्रीः प्रातःसवने. त्रिष्टुभो माध्यंदिनेऽथ कस्माज्जागते सति तृतीयसवने त्रिष्टुभोऽन्वाहेति । (*athāha yadyathācchaṃdasaṃ pūrvayoḥ savanayoranvāha gāyatrīḥ prātaḥsavane. triṣṭubho mādhyaṃdine 'tha kasmājjāgate sati tṛtīyasavane triṣṭubho 'nvāheti |*)

The connection of the Ṛbhus with Soma-drinking at the third savana has been stated. Now he examines a strange circumstance concerning the metre of the Unnīyamāna sūkta recited at the third savana. It has been stated earlier that the rule is that mantras in the Gāyatrī metre are to be recited at the Prātaḥsavana, mantras in the Triṣṭubh metre at the Mādhyandina-savana, and mantras in the Jagatī metre at the third savana. Accordingly it is proper that the Unnīyamāna sūkta be recited with Ṛks in the Gāyatrī metre at the Prātaḥsavana and with Ṛks in the Triṣṭubh metre at the Mādhyandina-savana. But in the Unnīyamāna sūkta recited at the third savana, इहोप यात (*ihopa yāta*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-35), there are no Ṛks in the Jagatī metre; there are only Ṛks in the Triṣṭubh metre. Is it not strange that, transgressing the Śruti sentence जागतं तृतीयसवनं (*jāgataṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ*), this Unnīyamāna sūkta इहोप यात (*ihopa yāta*) in the Triṣṭubh metre is recited? To this he gives the answer.

धीतरसं वै तृतीयसवनमथैतदधीतरसं शुक्रियं छंदो यत्रिष्टुप्सवनस्य. सरसताया इति ब्रूयादथो इंद्रमेवैतत्सवनेऽन्वाभजतीति । (*dhītarasaṃ vai tṛtīyasavanamathaitadadhītarasaṃ śukriyaṃ chaṃdo yatriṣṭupsavanasya. sarasatāyā iti brūyādatho iṃdramevaitatsavane 'nvābhajatīti |*)

Unlike the Prātaḥsavana and the Mādhyandina-savana, the Soma plants pounded at the third savana have no sap in them. Their sap was drunk long ago by Gāyatrī in the form of a falcon, and so it

### Printed p. 64

is sapless. How? Formerly Soma was in heaven. The gods thought that it must be brought to the earth for use in sacrifices, and devised a means. The metres such as Gāyatrī have the power to go to heaven and bring Soma. Therefore the gods begged the metres, Gāyatrī and the others, to bring Soma to the earth. Accepting the request, Gāyatrī and the other metres agreed to go to heaven. There is a narrative on this matter, which the commentator cites here:

जगत्युदपतच्चतुर्दशाक्षरासती साप्राप्य न्यवर्तत तस्यै द्वे अक्षरे अमीयेताग्ं सा पशुभिश्च दीक्षया चागच्छत्तस्माज्जगतीछंदसां पशव्यतमा तस्मात्पशुमंतं दीक्षोपनमति (*jagatyudapataccaturdaśākṣarāsatī sāprāpya nyavartata tasyai dve akṣare amīyetāgṃ sā paśubhiśca dīkṣayā cāgacchattasmājjagatīchaṃdasāṃ paśavyatamā tasmātpaśumaṃtaṃ dīkṣopanamati*)

त्रिष्टुगुदपतत्रयोदशाक्षरासती साप्राप्य न्यवर्तत तस्यै द्वे अक्षरे अमीयेताग्ं सा दक्षिणाभिश्च तपसा चागच्छत्तस्मात्रिष्टुभो लोके माध्यंदिने सवने दक्षिणानीयंत एतत्खलु वाव तप इत्याहुर्यः स्वं ददातीति (*triṣṭugudapatatrayodaśākṣarāsatī sāprāpya nyavartata tasyai dve akṣare amīyetāgṃ sā dakṣiṇābhiśca tapasā cāgacchattasmātriṣṭubho loke mādhyaṃdine savane dakṣiṇānīyaṃta etatkhalu vāva tapa ityāhuryaḥ svaṃ dadātīti*)

गायत्र्युदपतच्चतुरक्षरा सत्यजया ज्योतिषा तमस्या अजाभ्यरुंधत तदजाया अजत्वग्ं सा सोमं चाहरच्चत्वरि चाक्षराणि साष्टाक्षरा समपद्यत ब्रह्मवादिनो वदंति कस्मात्सत्याद्दायत्री कनिष्ठा छंदसाग्ं सती यज्ञमुखं परीयायेति यदेवादः सोममाहरत्तस्माद्यज्ञमुखं पर्यैत्तस्मात्तेजस्विनीतमा पद्भ्यां द्वे सवने समगृह्णान्मुखेनैकं (*gāyatryudapataccaturakṣarā satyajayā jyotiṣā tamasyā ajābhyaruṃdhata tadajāyā ajatvagṃ sā somaṃ cāharaccatvari cākṣarāṇi sāṣṭākṣarā samapadyata brahmavādino vadaṃti kasmātsatyāddāyatrī kaniṣṭhā chaṃdasāgṃ satī yajñamukhaṃ parīyāyeti yadevādaḥ somamāharattasmādyajñamukhaṃ paryaittasmāttejasvinītamā padbhyāṃ dve savane samagṛhṇānmukhenaikaṃ*)

यन्मुखेन समगृह्णात्तदधयत्तस्माद्वे सवने शुक्रवती प्रातःसवनं च माध्यंदिनं च तस्मात्तृतीयसवनऋजीषमभिषुण्वंति धीशमिव हि मन्यंते । (*yanmukhena samagṛhṇāttadadhayattasmādve savane śukravatī prātaḥsavanaṃ ca mādhyaṃdinaṃ ca tasmāttṛtīyasavanaṛjīṣamabhiṣuṇvaṃti dhīśamiva hi manyaṃte |*)

(Kai. Saṃ. 6-1-6-2, 3, 4) **NOTE**

First the Jagatī metre said that it would go and leaped into the atmosphere. But before it had gone far its strength failed, and having no power to go further it had to turn back. Thereby the Jagatī metre lost two of the fourteen syllables it had had in each pāda. Therefore each pāda of the Jagatī metre now has only twelve syllables. While returning in this way, unable to go further, that Jagatī metre brought with it the animals, which are a means of sacrifice, and the dīkṣā **NOTE**.

Therefore the Jagatī metre has the power to give animals. If the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice desires wealth in animals, then, when he is prepared in the sacrifice by the dīkṣā, he is prepared with mantras in the Jagatī metre. Then the Triṣṭubh metre said that it would go and leaped into the atmosphere. Before it had gone far it too grew weary and had to turn back. Then

### Printed p. 65

since the Triṣṭubh metre lost two of the thirteen syllables in its pāda, a pāda of the Triṣṭubh metre now has only eleven syllables. When returning, this Triṣṭubh metre brought with it from heaven the dakṣiṇā and the tapas, which are means of the sacrifice. Therefore even now, in the custom of the world, when a sacrifice is performed, the dakṣiṇā is given to the Ṛtvijs at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana. The giving away of wealth earned by oneself in this way is called tapas. Then the Gāyatrī said that it would go, and in the form of a falcon leaped up and flew into the atmosphere. While going it took with it, for help, a she-goat. When it went, the pādas of the Gāyatrī metre had only four syllables. Although among all the metres Gāyatrī is small in the measure of its syllables, by its own power it reached heaven, overcame the guards there and brought Soma. While bringing the Soma juice it brought back the two syllables each that the Jagatī and Triṣṭubh metres had lost. Those four syllables joined the four syllables that the Gāyatrī metre had before, and each pāda of the Gāyatrī became eight syllables. Because it brought Soma from heaven, Gāyatrī, though small in size, gained greater excellence. While bringing Soma to the earth, Gāyatrī in the form of the falcon held the Soma in its two feet and brought another portion gripped in its beak. The portion of Soma plants held in the two feet became the Prātaḥsavana and the Mādhyandina-savana. The portion brought gripped in the beak became the third savana. While bringing it in the beak the falcon bit the Soma plant and sucked out the juice, and so the Soma juice in those plants was not abundant and they became sapless. That is why it is said here that the third savana is not full of sap but sapless. Even now, at the third savana the Soma plants are not freshly pounded and pressed; rather, the husk-like plants left after the juice was pressed out at the time of the Mādhyandina-savana are soaked in water and the juice is pressed out again. Since there is not much juice in these husks, they make a connection with the Mādhyandina in order to increase its juice; that is, the mantras in the Triṣṭubh metre used at the Mādhyandina-savana are recited also at the third savana, in the Unnīyamāna sūkta. How? Just as a fire that has gone out, or is going out, is made to burn well by putting fresh pieces of wood on it, so, in order that the juice may come out more abundantly in the husks used for juice at the third savana, Ṛks in the Triṣṭubh metre are recited at the third savana as at the Mādhyandina-savana, and a connection with the Mādhyandina is established for it. This establishing of a connection is called शुक्रियं (*śukriyaṃ*); that is, it means rekindling what has gone out. Thus, for rekindling at the third savana, and so that the juice may come out more abundantly in the husks, and because the Triṣṭubh metre is dear to Indra, so as to do good to Indra and to establish a connection with Indra also at the third savana, the Unnīyamāna sūkta, which consists of Ṛks in the Triṣṭubh metre,

### Printed p. 66

is recited at the third savana. This is the reason for not using Ṛks in the Jagatī metre.

अथाह यदैंद्रार्भवं वै तृतीयसवनमथ कस्मादेष एव तृतीयसवने प्रस्थितानां प्रत्यक्षाद्यैंद्रार्भव्या यजतींद्र ऋभुभिर्वाजवद्भिः सुमुक्षितमिति होतैव नानादेवत्याभिरितरे कथं तेषामैंद्रार्भवो भवंतीति । (*athāha yadaiṃdrārbhavaṃ vai tṛtīyasavanamatha kasmādeṣa eva tṛtīyasavane prasthitānāṃ pratyakṣādyaiṃdrārbhavyā yajatīṃdra ṛbhubhirvājavadbhiḥ sumukṣitamiti hotaiva nānādevatyābhiritare kathaṃ teṣāmaiṃdrārbhavo bhavaṃtīti |*)

Having so far answered the objection concerning the use of mantras in the Triṣṭubh metre in place of the Jagatī metre in the Unnīyamāna sūkta recited for the third savana, he now answers the doubts and the like that arise concerning the prasthita yājyā Ṛks. It was said before that, after the recitation of the Unnīyamāna sūkta, at the third savana the seven Ṛtvijs, the Hotṛ and the others, recite the prasthita yājyā mantras in Aindrārbhava form, that is, with Ṛks having Indra and the Ṛbhus as their deities. But at the time of reciting the prasthita yājyā mantras the Ṛtvij called Hotṛ recites the yājyā mantra इंद्र ऋभुभिर्वाजवद्भिः (*iṃdra ṛbhubhirvājavadbhiḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-60-5), which contains the words Indra and Ṛbhu and has Indra and the Ṛbhus as its deities. For in the yājyā mantra recited by the Hotṛ the words इंद्रऋभुभिः (*iṃdraṛbhubhiḥ*) are used. The Hotṛ alone recites and offers with such a mantra of two deities. But the other six Ṛtvijs do not do so; they recite and offer with mantras of various deities. Therefore how can the yājyā mantras recited by these six Ṛtvijs become Aindrārbhava mantras? Since Indra and the Ṛbhus are not mentioned in their mantras, how will their yājyā mantras praise Indra and the Ṛbhus, the chief deities at the third savana? Is this not strange?

इंद्रावरुणा सुतपाविमं सुतमिति मैत्रावरुणो यजति युवो रथो अध्वरं देवनीतॆय इति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*iṃdrāvaruṇā sutapāvimaṃ sutamiti maitrāvaruṇo yajati yuvo ratho adhvaraṃ devanītèya iti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

It was said before that the Hotṛ recites the yājyā mantra इंद्र ऋभुभिर्वाजवद्भिः (*iṃdra ṛbhubhirvājavadbhiḥ*), which has Indra and the Ṛbhus as its deities. Now he explains the presence of the Aindrārbhava nature, that is, praise of Indra and the Ṛbhus, in the yājyā mantras recited by the other Ṛtvijs. First, the Maitrāvaruṇa recites as his yājyā mantra the Ṛk इंद्रावरुणा सुतपाविमं (*iṃdrāvaruṇā sutapāvimaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-68-10). In the third pāda of this Ṛk, युवो रथो अध्वरं देववीतये (*yuvo ratho adhvaraṃ devavītaye*), there is the word देववीतये (*devavītaye*). In the compounding of this word:

देवानां वीतिः प्राप्तिर्देववीतिरिति तस्य समासः । तस्य समासे षष्ठिबहुवचनत्वेन शब्देन बहून्येव रूपाणि प्रतीयंते । तत्र बहुत्वमृभूणां स्वरूपं । (*devānāṃ vītiḥ prāptirdevavītiriti tasya samāsaḥ | tasya samāse ṣaṣṭhibahuvacanatvena śabdena bahūnyeva rūpāṇi pratīyaṃte | tatra bahutvamṛbhūṇāṃ svarūpaṃ |*)

Since this is said, this word means "for the attainment of the gods". The word "of the gods" is the genitive plural. Since in this Ṛk the word Indra is directly present, the word "of the gods", conveyed by the plural word, applies to the Ṛbhus, who are three in number

### Printed p. 67

Therefore it must be understood that in this Ṛk both Indra and the Ṛbhus are praised.

इंद्रश्च सोमं पिबतं बृहस्पत इति ब्राह्मणाच्छंसी यजत्या नां विशंत्विंदवः स्वाभुव इति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*iṃdraśca somaṃ pibataṃ bṛhaspata iti brāhmaṇācchaṃsī yajatyā nāṃ viśaṃtviṃdavaḥ svābhuva iti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

The Brāhmaṇācchaṃsī recites the Ṛk इंद्रश्च सोमं पिबतं बृहस्पते (*iṃdraśca somaṃ pibataṃ bṛhaspate*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-50-10). In this Ṛk too the word Indra is used. In the third pāda of this Ṛk, आ वां विशंत्विंदवः स्वाभुवः (*ā vāṃ viśaṃtviṃdavaḥ svābhuvaḥ*), the plural word इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*) applies to the Ṛbhus, and so in this Ṛk too the Aindrārbhava nature is present.

आ वो वहंतु सप्तयो रघुष्यद इति पोता यजति रघुपत्वानः प्र जिगात बाहुभिरिति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*ā vo vahaṃtu saptayo raghuṣyada iti potā yajati raghupatvānaḥ pra jigāta bāhubhiriti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

The Ṛtvij called Potṛ recites the Ṛk आ वो वहंतु सप्तयो रघुष्यदः (*ā vo vahaṃtu saptayo raghuṣyadaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-85-6). In the second pāda of this Ṛk there is the plural-ending word रघुपत्वानः (*raghupatvānaḥ*), and there are also other plural-ending words, so it must be understood that the use of plural words indicates the Ṛbhus too.

अमेव नः सुहवा आ हि गंतनेति नेष्टा यजति गंतनेति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*ameva naḥ suhavā ā hi gaṃtaneti neṣṭā yajati gaṃtaneti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

The Ṛtvij called Neṣṭṛ recites and offers the Ṛk अमेव नः सुहवा आ हि गंतन (*ameva naḥ suhavā ā hi gaṃtana*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-36-3). In this yājyā mantra too, since there is the word गंतन (*gaṃtana*), a second-person plural word **NOTE**, it must be understood that in this Ṛk too the Aindrārbhava exposition is present.

इंद्राविष्णू पिबतं मध्वो अस्येत्यच्छावाको यजत्यानामंधांसि मदिराण्यग्मन्निति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*iṃdrāviṣṇū pibataṃ madhvo asyetyacchāvāko yajatyānāmaṃdhāṃsi madirāṇyagmanniti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

The Ṛtvij called Acchāvāka recites and offers the yājyā mantra इंद्राविष्णू पिबतं मध्वो अस्य (*iṃdrāviṣṇū pibataṃ madhvo asya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-70-7). Since in the third pāda of this Ṛk there are used plural-ending words such as अंधांसि (*aṃdhāṃsi*), here too the plural indicates the Ṛbhus.

इमं स्तोममर्हते जातवेदस इत्याग्नीध्रो यजति रथमिव सं महेमा मनीषयेति बहूनि वाह तदृभूणां रूपं । (*imaṃ stomamarhate jātavedasa ityāgnīdhro yajati rathamiva saṃ mahemā manīṣayeti bahūni vāha tadṛbhūṇāṃ rūpaṃ |*)

### Printed p. 68

The Ṛtvij called Āgnīdhra recites and offers the Ṛk इमं स्तोममर्हते जातवेदसे (*imaṃ stomamarhate jātavedase*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-94-1). Since in the second pāda of this Ṛk there are plural-ending words such as महेम (*mahema*), it must be understood that this mantra sets forth the nature of the Ṛbhus.

एवमु हैता ऐंद्रार्भव्यो भवंति यन्नानादेवात्यास्तेनान्या देवताः प्रीणाति । (*evamu haitā aiṃdrārbhavyo bhavaṃti yannānādevātyāstenānyā devatāḥ prīṇāti |*)

In this way the seven yājyā mantras spoken of before not only set forth the deities Indra and the Ṛbhus but, being of various deities, also please the various deities.

यदु जगत्प्रासहा जागतं वै तृतीयसवनं तृतीयसवनस्यैव समृध्यॆ ॥ (*yadu jagatprāsahā jāgataṃ vai tṛtīyasavanaṃ tṛtīyasavanasyaiva samṛdhyè ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 28-12)

Here jagat means the Jagatī metre. The word प्रासहा (*prāsahā*) indicates plurality. Therefore जगत्प्रासहा (*jagatprāsahā*) means a sūkta or mantras that have the Jagatī metre and plural-ending words. Since mantras of this kind are present both in the Unnīyamāna sūkta of the third savana and in the yājyā mantras, it is said जागतं वै तृतीयसवनं (*jāgataṃ vai tṛtīyasavanaṃ*): the third savana is of the nature of the Jagatī metre. The rite of the third savana, carried out with such a sūkta and such Ṛks, brings prosperity to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice.

Here the third savana ends.

**Discussion of the Soma Plant**

We have given extensive explanations of Soma many times before. Yet it is now necessary to give a more careful explanation of it. In the Veda there are many mantras concerning Soma. The whole ninth maṇḍala is praise of Soma. When many deities are invoked, they are called to come to this sacrifice for the drinking of Soma. There are many sacrifices connected with Soma. Among them the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice is the chief. In this sacrifice, with the recitation of mantras such as the stotra and the śastra, Soma is extracted according to rule at all three savanas (morning, noon and evening), prepared, and drunk by the sacrificer and the Ṛtvijs. In the other Soma sacrifices too this same order is followed, with some differences. Now we must examine what Soma is. Soma is a particular kind of creeper. This Soma plant is not found anywhere in India now. It grows especially on plains, on river banks and on mountains. The higher it grows, the more

### Printed p. 69

juice it gives. It grows even at a height of about 7000 feet; therefore there are, in many places in the Ṛgveda, descriptions of this Soma having been brought from the mountains (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-93-6; 3-48-2; 5-43-4, etc.). The mountain called Maujavata is famous for the Soma plant (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-34-1; Vā. Saṃ. 3-61-1; Ni. 9-8, etc.). It is said here and there in the Ṛgveda (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-7-29, 8-64-11, 9-65-22, etc.) that the Soma plant is found on the banks of the lake called Śaryaṇāvat. It is also said that Soma is found in the place called Ārjīka (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-7-29, 8-64-11, etc.), in the country called Pastyā (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-65-3), in the place where the five tribes called the Pañcajanas live (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-65-23, 9-92-3, etc.), and in the regions around the river called Suṣomā (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-7-29, 8-64-11, etc.). As to the last, Suṣomā, since it means su + soma, a place where good Soma is found, this region may have received the name Suṣoma. Therefore it is known that in the time of the Ṛgveda the Soma plant grew abundantly on the plains and river banks of what is now the Punjab. Since the plant growing on the plains did not yield as much juice as the plant growing on the mountains, they used the plant of the plains after soaking it well in water (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-63-4, 9-74-9, 9-79-4, etc.).

The stem of this plant grows to a height of about 4 feet. Its stem is round, soft and of a reddish colour mixed with white, and about a finger thick. The leaves are very few, white and small. Generally the leaves fall off. At the top of the plant there is a tuber (like an ear of sorghum) with some seeds. Its juice is white mixed with green. In taste it is slightly sweet, but if kept for some days it turns sour. When it grows near a big tree it spreads around the tree like a creeper. It has joints here and there. It can be broken easily.

We shall briefly describe here the procedure to be followed when preparing the Soma juice. The Soma plant, which grew on river banks and on mountains, was brought with great effort, or bought from those who had brought it, and used in sacrifices. Even now the rite called the purchase of Soma is performed in sacrifices. After the Soma plant is brought, it is pounded with stones (grāva, adri), pressed, and strained through a cloth; then, as the occasion requires, curds, milk and flour are mixed with it, it is offered to the deities, and afterwards the sacrificer and the Ṛtvijs drink it; this is the custom. This preparation of the Soma juice takes place on the principal days of the sacrifice, in the morning, at noon and in the evening. It is called abhiṣavaṇa or savana (extracting the juice). The savana performed in the evening is called the third savana.

### Printed p. 70

The Soma plant (the creepers) is of a brown, reddish (russet) or yellowish-red colour. Since it is said that the plant grown very high on the mountains is the best and that more juice can be extracted from it, they brought the Soma plant from the mountains themselves wherever possible. Among such mountains the one called Mūjavata was famous. The shoot of this Soma plant has the name अंस (*aṃsa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-168-3; 3-48-2, etc.); a shoot with sprouts is called वक्षणा (*vakṣaṇā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-1-17) and वाण (*vāṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-28-9; 9-50-1); the Soma juice is called अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28-7; 3-48-1; 4-16 1) **NOTE**; and the stalk (creeper) of the Soma plant is called पर्वन् (*parvan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-9-1); this is the usage.

**The Procedure for Preparing the Soma Juice**

The leaves on the Soma plant or creeper are removed, and it is put in a mortar and pounded with a stone. If the plant is not fresh, it is soaked in water for a day or two, and when it has become soft the juice is extracted. The mortar in which the Soma plant is pounded is called ulūkhala. It is generally made of stone; it is sometimes made of wood. The stone that is put into the mortar and pounded from above has the names grāvā (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-83-6; 1-135-7) and adri (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-130-2; 1-135-5; 1-137-1). It may be inferred that the ulūkhala was something like the mortar we use now, a stone with only a slight hollow in the middle. This mortar called ulūkhala was used for pounding grain with a pestle (musala).

The procedure of pounding the Soma plant and preparing the Soma juice is nowhere described at length in the Ṛgveda. The procedure can be inferred from some sentences stated here and there. The stones for pounding the Soma plants have the names grāvā and adri. In some cases wooden boards, known by the name adhiṣavaṇa board (phalaka), are also used. If the plant is fresh, more juice comes out. Otherwise, that is, if some days have passed since it was cut and brought, they are soaked in water and afterwards placed on a skin. Then they are pounded with stones and the juice is extracted. Since the juice extracted in this way is mixed with husks, bits and the like of the plant, they strain that juice either through the gaps between the fingers of the hands, or through a sieve or cloth called pavitra, or through a sheep's wool **NOTE**. The juice strained in this way is clean and free of impurities. It has the names śukra and śuci. In appearance this juice is of a russet colour or a yellowish red. It is the custom to offer to Indra and to Vāyu pure Soma juice, that is, juice without any mixture of āśira substances. To the other deities it is sometimes used mixed with milk, curds and flour of grain. The Soma juice in which

### Printed p. 71

these are mixed has the names gavāśira, dadhyāśira and yavāśira respectively.

**Vessels for the Soma Juice**

The vessel in which Soma is placed for offering to the deities is called camū. The vessels the Ṛtvijs use for drinking Soma are called kalaśa and camasa. In some cases the word camū is also used to denote the mortar and the pounding stone (ulūkhala and grāvā, mortar and pestle) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28-9; 4-18-3; 6-5-2). When the Soma plant is pounded, it is the custom generally to place it on skins. This skin is called त्वक् (*tvak*). The hide of a cow was used for this (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-94-9; 10-116-4). The vessels in which Soma is collected and kept have names such as kośa (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-4; 8-20-8), sadastha (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-62-15; 9-1-2; 9-17-8), dru (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-1-2-; 9-65-6; 9-98-2), vana (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-14-9; 9-66-9) and droṇa (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-15-2; 9-33-2). Sruva means a ladle (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-24). The cloth or sieve that strains the Soma juice is called pavitra. In some cases they also strained the Soma through the gaps between the fingers. Soma juice used without mixture of other substances is called शुक्र (*śukra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-137-1; 3-32-2; 8-2-10) and शुचि (*śuci*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-5-5; 1-30-2; 8-2-9). There are various descriptions: that the colour of this juice is of many kinds, बभ्रु (*babhru*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-33-2; 9-63-4 and 6), हरित (*harita*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-3-9; 9-7-6; 9-65-5), अरुण (*aruṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-40-2; 9-45-3), अरुष (*aruṣa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-61-21) and शोण (*śoṇa*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-97-13), and that it is fragrant (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-97-9, 9-107-2).

To give the Soma juice taste, certain substances are mixed with it. This mixture is called āśira substances. Soma juice mixed with milk is called गवाशिर (*gavāśira*), that mixed with curds दध्याशिर (*dadhyāśira*), that mixed with flour यवाशिर (*yavāśira*). The Soma juice is sometimes said to be sharp (tīvra); it should then be understood to have a pungent smell or flavour. The husk-like part that remains after the Soma juice has been taken out is called ऋजीष (*ṛjīṣa*).

Grāvā: the stone that pounds the Soma juice. As the word पृथुबुध्नः (*pṛthubudhnaḥ*) explains, this stone is a round stone broad at the bottom and like a handle above; a stone shaped like the grinding stone in a stone mill

### Printed p. 72

In the Ṛgveda the stones used for pounding the Soma plant are generally called by the words grāvā and adri.

**Examples of Āśira Substances**

Dadhyāśira: for this word the commentator has explained अवनीयमानं दध्याशीर्दोषघातकं येषां सोमानां ते दध्याशिरः (*avanīyamānaṃ dadhyāśīrdoṣaghātakaṃ yeṣāṃ somānāṃ te dadhyāśiraḥ*), that is, because curds are mixed with it to remove the faults in the Soma juice, it is called dadhyāśira. It was the custom to use the Soma juice mixed (for taste) not only with curds but also with other substances such as milk and flour. We have written about this at length in this Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, Part 1, page 6:6. The word दध्याशिरः (*dadhyāśiraḥ*) occurs in 1-187-9 and in the other Ṛks given below:

(1) इम अयातमिंदवः सोमसो दध्याशिरः सुतासो दध्याशिरः । (*ima ayātamiṃdavaḥ somaso dadhyāśiraḥ sutāso dadhyāśiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-137-2) **GLOSS**

(2) सुता इंद्राय वायवे सोमसो दध्याशिरः । (*sutā iṃdrāya vāyave somaso dadhyāśiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-51-7) **GLOSS**

(3) इम इंद्राय सुन्विरे सोमासो दध्याशिरः । (*ima iṃdrāya sunvire somāso dadhyāśiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-32-4) **GLOSS**

(4) एते पूता विपश्चितः सोमासो दध्याशिरः । (*ete pūtā vipaścitaḥ somāso dadhyāśiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-22-3) **GLOSS**

(5) सुता इंद्राय वज्रिणे सोमासो दध्याशिरः । (*sutā iṃdrāya vajriṇe somāso dadhyāśiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-63-15) **GLOSS**

(6) एते पूता विपश्चितः सोमसो दध्याशिरः ॥ (*ete pūtā vipaścitaḥ somaso dadhyāśiraḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-101-12) **GLOSS**

Besides this word dadhyāśira, Soma juices mixed with other substances, such as gavāśira and yavāśira, are recited in these Ṛks:

(1) तीव्राः सोमास आ गह्याशीर्वंतः सुता इमे ॥ (*tīvrāḥ somāsa ā gahyāśīrvaṃtaḥ sutā ime ‖*) (1-23-1) **GLOSS**

Since this Ṛk says आशीर्वंतः सोमासः (*āśīrvaṃtaḥ somāsaḥ*), it is stated in general that some kind of āśira substance, such as milk or curds, has been mixed with the Soma juice.

(2) शतं वा यः शुचीनां सहस्रं ना समाशिरां ॥ (*śataṃ vā yaḥ śucīnāṃ sahasraṃ nā samāśirāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-30-2) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 73

Here समाशिरां (*samāśirāṃ*) is said in general to mean that āśira substances have been well mixed with the Soma juice. No particular substance is named.

(3) सुषुमा यातमदिभिर्गोश्रीता मत्सरा इमे सोमासो मत्सरा इमे। आ राजाना दिविस्पृशास्मत्र गंतमुप नः ॥ इमे नां मित्रावरुणा गवाशिरः सोमाः शुक्रा गवाशिरः ॥ (*suṣumā yātamadibhirgośrītā matsarā ime somāso matsarā ime| ā rājānā divispṛśāsmatra gaṃtamupa naḥ ‖ ime nāṃ mitrāvaruṇā gavāśiraḥ somāḥ śukrā gavāśiraḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-137-1) **GLOSS**

Here गोश्रीताः (*gośrītāḥ*) means mixed with the milk of cows; that is the meaning. The word गवाशिरः (*gavāśiraḥ*) in the last pāda of the same Ṛk also means Soma mixed with the milk of a cow.

(4) शुचिरसि पुरुनिःष्ठाः क्षीरैर्मध्यतः आशीर्तः । दध्ना मंदिष्ठः शूरस्य ॥ (*śucirasi puruniḥṣṭhāḥ kṣīrairmadhyataḥ āśīrtaḥ | dadhnā maṃdiṣṭhaḥ śūrasya ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-2-9) **GLOSS**

Here it is said that there are two different juices, one mixed with milk and one mixed with curds; that is, it should be understood that there are two kinds, the Soma juice mixed with milk and the Soma juice mixed with curds.

(5) सं शुक्रासः शुचयः सं गवाशिरः सोमा इंद्रमंदिषुः ॥ (*saṃ śukrāsaḥ śucayaḥ saṃ gavāśiraḥ somā iṃdramaṃdiṣuḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-52-10) **GLOSS**

Here it is stated clearly that gavāśira means Soma mixed with milk.

(6) अधा नियुत्व उभयस्य नः पिब शुचिं सोमं गवाशिरं ॥ (*adhā niyutva ubhayasya naḥ piba śuciṃ somaṃ gavāśiraṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-101-10) **GLOSS**

Here too it is Soma mixed with milk, called gavāśira, that is meant.

(7) यत्ते सोम गवाशिरो भजामहे ॥ (*yatte soma gavāśiro bhajāmahe ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-187-9) **GLOSS**

(8) इमदिंद्र गवाशिरं च नः पिब । (*imadiṃdra gavāśiraṃ ca naḥ piba |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-42-7) **GLOSS**

Here two kinds of Soma juice are mentioned, gavāśira and yavāśira.

(9) इंदोरिंद्रो यवाशिरः (*iṃdoriṃdro yavāśiraḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-92-4) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 74

(10) त्रिकद्रुकेषु महिषो यवाशिरं तुविशुष्मस्तृपत्सोममपिबद्विष्णुना सुतं यथावशत् ॥ (*trikadrukeṣu mahiṣo yavāśiraṃ tuviśuṣmastṛpatsomamapibadviṣṇunā sutaṃ yathāvaśat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-22-1) **GLOSS**

Here it is said that yavāśira means Soma juice mixed with flour.

(11) अश्वमेधस्य दानाः सोमा इव त्र्याशिरः ॥ (*aśvamedhasya dānāḥ somā iva tryāśiraḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-27-5) **GLOSS**

Here it is said that three kinds of substances, milk, curds and barley (yava), are mixed with the Soma juice.

(12) साधोः पिब प्रतिकामं यथा ते रसाशिरः प्रथमं सोमस्य ॥ (*sādhoḥ piba pratikāmaṃ yathā te rasāśiraḥ prathamaṃ somasya ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-48-1) **GLOSS**

Since रसाशिर (*rasāśira*) is said here, it means Soma mixed with either milk or curds.

**The Method of Preparing the Soma Juice**

(As in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa)

The Aitareya Brāhmaṇa describes in the following way the implements to be used when pressing the Soma juice, and the order of pressing and purifying.

एतान्यस्य पुरुस्तादुपक्षृप्तानि भवंत्यथ सोमं राजानं क्रीणंति ते राज्य एवावृतौपवसथात्पतिनेशैश्चरंत्यथौपवसथ्यमहरेतान्यध्वर्युः पुरस्तादुप कल्पयेताधिषवणं चर्माधिषवणे फलके द्रोणकलशं दशापवित्रमद्रीन् पूतभृतं चाधवनीयं च स्थालीमुदंचनं चमसं च तद्यदेतद्राजानं प्रातरभिषुण्वंति तदेनानि द्वेधा विगृह्णीयादभ्यन्यानि सुनुयान्माध्यं दिनायान्यानि परिशिंष्यात् । (*etānyasya purustādupakṣṛptāni bhavaṃtyatha somaṃ rājānaṃ krīṇaṃti te rājya evāvṛtaupavasathātpatineśaiścaraṃtyathaupavasathyamaharetānyadhvaryuḥ purastādupa kalpayetādhiṣavaṇaṃ carmādhiṣavaṇe phalake droṇakalaśaṃ daśāpavitramadrīn pūtabhṛtaṃ cādhavanīyaṃ ca sthālīmudaṃcanaṃ camasaṃ ca tadyadetadrājānaṃ prātarabhiṣuṇvaṃti tadenāni dvedhā vigṛhṇīyādabhyanyāni sunuyānmādhyaṃ dināyānyāni pariśiṃṣyāt |*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-32.)

On the very first day on which the rite begins, the Adhvaryu must have ready all the implements needed for pressing out the juice, and then begin pressing the juice. The goat skin, droṇakalaśa, daśāpavitra, pūtabhṛt, ādhavanīya, sthālī, camasa, grāvā, adri and the like are the implements needed there. The procedure for obtaining the juice with these implements is as follows. (Or, among the variations of procedure, this may be one.)

The Adhvaryu spreads out a skin and places on it the Soma plant in the form of aṃśu (creeper). First he places the adhiṣavaṇa board (a plank or stone) on it and pounds with the grāvā; then

### Printed p. 75

he takes the pounded Soma plants out from under the board and pounds them together. Then he places another board on it, and with the water of the vasatīvarī vessel collected on the previous day he pours water on this board, and in the camasa vessel of the Hotṛ, which stands on the right, he stirs three times the Soma plant that has been pounded and soaked with vasatīvarī water. After this nigrābha procedure he dips it in the vasatīvarī vessel and wets it with water. Then he places it on the grāvā, spreads a little grass over them, and pounds with another grāvā to press out the juice. This way of pounding is called abhiṣavaṇa. In this procedure of abhiṣavaṇa there are three alternatives. In the first, as described above, the Adhvaryu pounds eight times and performs the nigrābha procedure; in the second alternative eleven times, and in the third twelve times. After this preliminary abhiṣava procedure has been performed, he also carries out the mahābhiṣava in the order stated above (as required for each savana only) and pours the juice pressed out into the ādhavanīya vessel. Then he strains it through the cloth called daśāpavitra and passes it into the vessel called pūtabhṛt beneath it. At this time the Udgātṛ must hold this cloth called daśāpavitra.

In the Ṛk 1-135-5, for the word मर्मृजंत (*marmṛjaṃta*), he has given the statement अध्वर्यवो दशापवित्रेण संमार्जयंति (*adhvaryavo daśāpavitreṇa saṃmārjayaṃti*): the Adhvaryus purify with the daśāpavitra; thus the matter described above has been indicated.

What is said here as आद्रिभिः सुतानां (*ādribhiḥ sutānāṃ*) is described in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā in this way:

आ ददे ग्रावास्यध्वरकृद्देवेभ्यो गंभीरमिममध्वरं कृध्युत्तमेन पविनेंद्राय सोमग्‌० सुषुतं मधुमंतं पयस्वंतं वृष्टिवनिं । (*ā dade grāvāsyadhvarakṛddevebhyo gaṃbhīramimamadhvaraṃ kṛdhyuttamena pavineṃdrāya somag0 suṣutaṃ madhumaṃtaṃ payasvaṃtaṃ vṛṣṭivaniṃ |*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 1-4-1.)

O grāvā for pressing, which is the means for the gods to carry out the sacrifice (upāṃśugraha or upāṃśusavana), I have taken you in order to press the Soma juice. With your sharp point press for Indra the Soma that is full of sweetness, full of juice and bringer of rain, and make the sacrifice deep. [With this mantra the grāvā (the pounding stone) is to be taken.]

इंद्राय त्वा वृत्रघ्न इंद्राय त्वा वृत्रतुर इंद्राय त्वाभिमातिघ्न इंद्राय त्वादित्यवत इंद्राय त्वा विश्वदेव्यावते ॥ (*iṃdrāya tvā vṛtraghna iṃdrāya tvā vṛtratura iṃdrāya tvābhimātighna iṃdrāya tvādityavata iṃdrāya tvā viśvadevyāvate ‖*)

O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, this is offered to you; O Indra, conqueror of Vṛtra, this is for you; O Indra, slayer of enemies, this is for you; O Indra, accompanied by the Ādityas, this is for you; O Indra, accompanied by the Viśvedevas, this is for you. [With this mantra five separate offerings for the first oblation are to be indicated and measured]—

### Printed p. 76

श्वात्राः स्थ वृत्रतुरो राधोगूर्ता अमृतस्य पत्निस्ता देवीर्देवत्रेमं यज्ञं धत्तोपहूताः सोमस्य पिबतोपहूतो युष्माकं सोमः पिबतु ॥ (*śvātrāḥ stha vṛtraturo rādhogūrtā amṛtasya patnistā devīrdevatremaṃ yajñaṃ dhattopahūtāḥ somasya pibatopahūto yuṣmākaṃ somaḥ pibatu ‖*)

O you waters, full of sap, conquerors of Vṛtra, wives of the immortal, make glad by your gifts and the rest, and establish this our sacrifice among the gods. Invited by us, drink the Soma juice; and let Soma, invited by you, drink also. [With this mantra water is to be sprinkled on the Soma plant.]

यत्ते सोम दिवि ज्योतिर्यत्पृथिव्यां यदुरावंतरिक्षे तेनास्मै यजमानायोरु ! राया कृध्यधि दात्रे वोचः । (*yatte soma divi jyotiryatpṛthivyāṃ yadurāvaṃtarikṣe tenāsmai yajamānāyoru ! rāyā kṛdhyadhi dātre vocaḥ |*)

O Soma, with that splendour of yours which spreads in the heaven, upon the earth and in the wide atmosphere, bestow wealth in abundance upon this sacrificer; show favour to the giver of the oblation. [With this mantra water is to be mixed with the Soma.]

धिषणे वीडू सती वीडयेथामूर्जं दधाथामूर्जं मे धत्तं मा वाग्ं हिग्ंसिषं मा मा हिग्ंसिष्टं । (*dhiṣaṇe vīḍū satī vīḍayethāmūrjaṃ dadhāthāmūrjaṃ me dhattaṃ mā vāgṃ higṃsiṣaṃ mā mā higṃsiṣṭaṃ |*)

O mighty Dhiṣaṇas, let your strength increase. Gather strength and give strength to me also. I shall not injure you; do not injure me. [With this mantra the pressing-boards are to be touched.]

प्रागपागुवदगधराक्तास्ता दिश आ धानत्वंब निष्वर । (*prāgapāguvadagadharāktāstā diśa ā dhānatvaṃba niṣvara |*)

May the intervening spaces of the directions, those in front, behind, above and below, urge you on; O mother, come out quickly. [This mantra belongs to the nigrābha procedure. The Unnetṛ brings water and pours it into the vessel of the Hotṛ; the Soma juice also falls with it.]

यत्ते सोमादाभ्यं नाम जागृवि तस्मै ते सोमःसोमाय स्वाहा ॥ (*yatte somādābhyaṃ nāma jāgṛvi tasmai te somaḥsomāya svāhā ‖*)

[With this mantra the Pratiprasthātṛ puts down the six Soma plants which he had taken.]

After the Soma juice had thus been produced and purified, Indra and Vāyu were invited to come to drink it. This invitation stands in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā as follows.

आ वायो भूष शुचिपा उप नः सहस्रं ते नियुतो विश्ववार । उपो ते अंधो मद्यमयामि यस्य देव दधिषे पूर्वपेयं । उपयामगृही- तोऽसि वायवे त्वेंद्रवायू इमे सुताः । उप प्रयोभिरागतमिंदवो नामुशंति हि । उपयामगृहीतोऽसींद्रवायुभ्यां त्वैष ते योनिः सजोषाभ्यां त्वा ॥ (*ā vāyo bhūṣa śucipā upa naḥ sahasraṃ te niyuto viśvavāra | upo te aṃdho madyamayāmi yasya deva dadhiṣe pūrvapeyaṃ | upayāmagṛhī- to 'si vāyave tveṃdravāyū ime sutāḥ | upa prayobhirāgatamiṃdavo nāmuśaṃti hi | upayāmagṛhīto 'sīṃdravāyubhyāṃ tvaiṣa te yoniḥ sajoṣābhyāṃ tvā ‖*)

### Printed p. 77

(Tai. Saṃ. 1-4-4)

O Vāyu, who drink the pure juice, be gracious with your thousand horses. For your first drink this sweet Soma juice has been pressed and made ready. O Indra and Vāyu, these Soma juices desire your coming. Come together, as companions of each other, and drink this juice prepared and purified for you: so the invitation was made.

After the pressing, the procedure by which the Adhvaryus are to prepare this juice for the offering, and the mantras to be recited at that time, are given in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā in this way. It is called the Upāṃśugraha. (Tai. Saṃ. 1-4-2)

1. वाचस्पतये पवस्व वाजिन्वृषा वृष्णो अग्‌ंशुभ्यां गभस्तिपूतो देवो देवानां पवित्रमसि येषां भागोऽसि तेभ्यस्त्वा (*vācaspataye pavasva vājinvṛṣā vṛṣṇo agṃśubhyāṃ gabhastipūto devo devānāṃ pavitramasi yeṣāṃ bhāgo 'si tebhyastvā*)

हे वाजिन्नन्नप्रद सोम पतये पालकदेवार्थं वाचः संबंधिना मंत्रेण पवस्व शुद्धो भव । (*he vājinnannaprada soma pataye pālakadevārthaṃ vācaḥ saṃbaṃdhinā maṃtreṇa pavasva śuddho bhava |*)

O Soma, giver of food, for the sake of the protecting god become pure through the mantras connected with speech. Before this you were purified in the forest by the rays of the sun; now be purified again by the Soma shoots that have been placed within. Being the best among the gods, you are the cause of purification also for him who drinks you. Now I take you to those gods for whom you are appointed. [In this part of the rite called Upāṃśugraha, the Adhvaryu pours the juice into the vessel held by the Pratiprasthātṛ. In that vessel there are two parts, and the juice is to be made pure. This act is to be carried out while reciting the portion of the mantra beginning वाचस्पतये पवस्व (*vācaspataye pavasva*).]

2. स्वां कृतोऽसि मधुमतीर्न इषस्कृधि विश्वेभ्यस्त्वेंद्रियेभ्यो दिव्येभ्यःपार्थिवेभ्यः । (*svāṃ kṛto 'si madhumatīrna iṣaskṛdhi viśvebhyastveṃdriyebhyo divyebhyaḥpārthivebhyaḥ |*)

O Upāṃśugraha, you have been accepted by us. Make our food full of sweetness. I accept you for the increase of all the powers that are in the divine world and in the earthly world. [With this mantra the vessel is to be taken, looked at, and rubbed in the left hand.]

3. मनस्त्वाष्टु (*manastvāṣṭu*)—may the mind pervade you. [With this mantra there is the upasthāna.]

### Printed p. 78

4. उर्वंतरिक्षमन्विहि (*urvaṃtarikṣamanvihi*)—crossing the wide atmosphere, be gracious and come to the region of the Āhavanīya. [Reciting this mantra, the Adhvaryu comes to the region of the Āhavanīya.]

5. स्वाहा त्वा सुभवः सूर्याय (*svāhā tvā subhavaḥ sūryāya*)—(सूर्याय स्वाहा (*sūryāya svāhā*)) [With this mantra the juice is offered in the fire.]

(स्वाहा (*svāhā*)) देवेभ्यस्ता मरीचिपेभ्यः (*devebhyastā marīcipebhyaḥ*)—[This too follows the rule above.] This is one order; or else, reciting स्वाहा त्वा सुभवः सूर्याय देवेभ्यस्ता मरीचिपेभ्यः (*svāhā tvā subhavaḥ sūryāya devebhyastā marīcipebhyaḥ*) together and offering is another order.

7. एष ते योनिः प्राणाय त्वा (*eṣa te yoniḥ prāṇāya tvā*)—this is your place of origin. [Reciting this mantra, the remainder is poured into the Āgrayaṇa vessel, and a plant is put into the emptied vessel.]**NOTE**

The same procedure, with some variation, is stated in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa by the sentences beginning अथ राजानमुपावहरति (*atha rājānamupāvaharati*) इयं वै प्रतिष्ठा (*iyaṃ vai pratiṣṭhā*) (Śa. Brā. 3-9-3-2 to 33) and so on; they tell the duties of the Adhvaryu and how he prepares the Soma juice for the oblation and carries it away.**NOTE**

शुक्राः (*śukrāḥ*)—शुभ्राः दीपाः (*śubhrāḥ dīpāḥ*). It is explained as bright, shining.

**The explanation of the word pavitra**

पवित्रं (*pavitraṃ*)—it is difficult to state the exact meaning of the word pavitra. ऊर्णामयं पवित्रं सोमांतर्धायकं (*ūrṇāmayaṃ pavitraṃ somāṃtardhāyakaṃ*): that is, if this pavitra made of ūrṇā (wool) is placed within the Soma juice, purification results; this is one procedure. Or else, making it into a sieve-like instrument for filtering, pouring the Soma juice into it and purifying it is another procedure. Probably the straining through a sieve is what is indicated here.

The authors of the Nirukta have given different meanings of this word. But to show that the purpose is one, having explained that it is called pavitra because it purifies, पवित्रं पुनातेः (*pavitraṃ punāteḥ*) (Ni. 5-6),

1. मंत्रः पवित्रमुच्यते (*maṃtraḥ pavitramucyate*): the mantra is called pavitra; quoting the sentence येन देवाः पवित्रेणात्मानं पुनते सदा (*yena devāḥ pavitreṇātmānaṃ punate sadā*) (Sā. Ve. 5-2-8-5; 2-652) they take the meaning there to be mantra,**NOTE**

2. रश्मयः पवित्रमुच्यते (*raśmayaḥ pavitramucyate*): the rays also are called pavitra.

3. अपः पवित्रमुच्यंते (*apaḥ pavitramucyaṃte*): the waters also are called pavitra; thus,

### Printed p. 79

quoting the Ṛk शतपवित्राः स्वधया मदंतीर्देवीर्देवानामपि यंति साथि (*śatapavitrāḥ svadhayā madaṃtīrdevīrdevānāmapi yaṃti sāthi*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-87-3) they have shown that there it means water. But in the present Ṛk the sense is purifying, and none of the water, ray or mantra stated by the Nirukta authors fits. The meaning is an instrument of the nature of a sieve. Sometimes a bundle of darbha grass is placed over the vessel and the juice is poured over it to purify it. Since it is here explained as made of wool, it must be a sieve made of fleece.

In the Ṛk उच्चिष्टं चन्नोर्भर सोमं पवित्र आ सृज । नि धेहि गोरधि त्वचि ॥ (*ucciṣṭaṃ cannorbhara somaṃ pavitra ā sṛja | ni dhehi goradhi tvaci ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-28-9) it is explained as daśāpavitra. (This word was explained in the previous Ṛk.) The daśāpavitra is merely of cloth. Here, since अव्यया रोमाणि अति (*avyayā romāṇi ati*), that is, they pour down beyond the hairs that are not split, it is understood to be the fleece of a sheep. Sāyaṇa has explained it as अविरोमाणि (*aviromāṇi*).

अन्ये वधूयुः पवते परि त्वचि श्रथ्नीते नप्तीरदितेर्युतं यते । हरिरक्रान् यजतः संयतो मदो नृम्मा शिशानो महिषो न शोभते ॥ (*anye vadhūyuḥ pavate pari tvaci śrathnīte naptīraditeryutaṃ yate | harirakrān yajataḥ saṃyato mado nṛmmā śiśāno mahiṣo na śobhate ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-69-3)

In Ṛks such as this, just as अव्ये त्वचि (*avye tvaci*) is explained as अवीः स्वभूते चर्मणि (*avīḥ svabhūte carmaṇi*), so here too the word अव्य (*avya*) is explained as relating to a sheep, and अव्ययाः सोमासः (*avyayāḥ somāsaḥ*) is to be understood as meaning that the juice goes through this fleece, falls and makes a sound. This same point, described in this Ṛk by अव्यया रोमाणि अति (*avyayā romāṇi ati*),

प्र ते धारा अत्यण्णानि मेष्यः पुनानस्य संयतो यंति रंहयः । यद्योभिरिंदो चन्नोः समज्यस अ सुवानः सोम कलशेषु सीदसि ॥ (*pra te dhārā atyaṇṇāni meṣyaḥ punānasya saṃyato yaṃti raṃhayaḥ | yadyobhiriṃdo cannoḥ samajyasa a suvānaḥ soma kalaśeṣu sīdasi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-86-47)

is stated there too, in the same way, as the manner of purifying the Soma juice. रंहयः धाराः मेष्यः अण्णानि अणूनि लोमानि अतियंति । (*raṃhayaḥ dhārāḥ meṣyaḥ aṇṇāni aṇūni lomāni atiyaṃti |*): it is explained just as it is described here, that the streams of juice flow through the fine fleece. The word अव्ययाः (*avyayāḥ*) is repeated, and the second time it is an epithet of सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*). There it means without loss, that is, without decay.

### Printed p. 80

**Indra's regard for Soma-drinking**

As soon as he is invited by the sacrificers for the drinking of Soma, Indra mounts his chariot and comes ready. There too food for his horses is ready. And as soon as he has come, an oblation in the form of Soma is made ready in abundance and offered for his drinking. (3-35-7)

Immediately after this Soma-drinking he attains exceeding joy. And from it he obtains not only the eagerness required for battle but also strength useful for many other extraordinary works, such as the upholding of heaven and earth. All kinds of strength and eagerness increase. (2-15-2)

Of the many sentences that tell the manner of Indra's worship, only the purport of a few is stated here. This Soma juice produces imperishable vigour. The seer prays that, just as the sun pervades the atmosphere and the other regions with his rays, this Soma juice may make strength pervade the whole body of Indra.

(2-15-1, 2; 2-19-1; 3-36-3; 3-40-1, 3-42-1; 6-23-1, 5, 6; 6-27-1; 6-29-4; 7-22-1; 7-29-1; 8-3-1; 8-81-5, 6; 10-103-1)

All the gods run eagerly to the portion of Soma juice offered to Indra. (8-2-19)

"Together with Indra, the other gods and Agni have drunk. All the gods have been satisfied. But the satisfaction of Indra is the greatest." (1-184-9; 1-175-5; 2-14-1; 3-33-2; 8-2-4)

Among all the other gods, the Soma-drinking of Indra is the most exceeding. इंद्र इत् सोमपा एकः........अंतर्देवान् मर्त्यांश्च । (*iṃdra it somapā ekaḥ........aṃtardevān martyāṃśca |*) (8-2-4): among gods and men and the rest, Indra alone is truly worthy of drinking Soma. Therefore he alone is truly the possessor of heroes. (8-4-12; 8-6-40)

Since Indra's mother provided him with Soma on the very day of his birth, it is clear that Soma is life for Indra. (सद्यो यज्ञातो अपिबो ह सोमं (*sadyo yajñāto apibo ha somaṃ*)). (3-32-9, 10; 3-48-2; 6-40-2; 7-98-3.)

It is famed that in a single draught he drank the Soma of thirty Droṇa vessels. (8-66-4; 6-17-11; 8-7-20. Ni. 4-11.)

### Printed p. 81

His devotees call him to this Soma-drinking with the utmost humility and devotion, and Indra, accepting their call and coming, drinks in haste like a thirsty deer. (8-4-10; 5-36-1; 8-33-2.)

The belly (bellies) of this Indra is like two lakes. To fill it Indra, in his eagerness, roars like a bull wandering in search of water in a waterless desert. 5-36-1; 1-8-7; 1-104-9; 2-11-11; 2-14-10; 2-16-2; 3-35-6; 3-36-7, 8; 3-40-5; 3-47-1; 3-51-12; 8-12-23; 8-2-1; 8-17-5 to 8; 8-67-7; 8-81 to 22-24; 10-25.2; 10-43-7; 10-104-2; A. Se. 2-5-1; 6-2-1).**NOTE**

Sometimes the same description occurs when the sacrificers themselves drink this Soma juice. For example:

"As soon as I have drunk this Soma, it increases my body" (6-47-3.)

so the seer declares. In another passage, telling the power of Soma-drinking, he proclaims:

"We have drunk Soma, therefore we have become free from death. No man can now injure us. O Indra, immortal one, by your grace we also, together with you, without fear, shall climb to the atmosphere." (8-48-3)

As birds fly to a tree full of leaves, and as rivers full of water go and join the ocean, so the stream of Soma juice goes and joins Indra. As, when rain falls, grain and the like grow abundant and strong, so by the offering of Soma juice the sacrificers cause Indra to grow strong. (19-43-4,7)**NOTE**

As a good husband loves his wife and desires her company, so Indra is prayed to drink the Soma offered by the sacrificers, to accept the praises offered by them, and to be satisfied by them. (3-52-3; 4-32-16).

In one sūkta, in order to show clearly how the power of Soma-drinking increases Indra's vigour, the seer has described the nature of Indra in this way; the Soma-drinking

### Printed p. 82

has satisfied Indra, and from this it is known in what various ways Indra, so satisfied, shows his favour to the sacrificer.

"I have drunk the Soma fully. Therefore I shall provide the sacrificer with cattle and horses. The stream of Soma that I have drunk has produced in me exceeding vigour. As a cow runs lovingly to her calf and joins it, the hymn composed by the seer has come and joined me. As the smith goes round the chariot-seat and fashions it, so I make that hymn pervade my heart. Even the five peoples, the Gandharvas and the rest, are small before me. That is the power of Soma. Even if heaven and earth were joined together they could not equal half of me. I am possessed of a greatness that surpasses the greatness of these two, heaven and earth. I establish this earth according to my resolve. The atmosphere is half of me; the other half I have brought down. I have the might to rule in the atmosphere. As the fruit of this Soma-drinking I have the power to take the oblation to all the gods and distribute it." (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-119-1 to 13)

Like the Soma offered to Indra, the hymns offered by the sacrificer and other kinds of worship also increase his valour and vigour exceedingly. ब्रह्माणींद्र तवयानि वर्धना (*brahmāṇīṃdra tavayāni vardhanā*) (उक्थैर्वर्धमानाः (*ukthairvardhamānāḥ*))

(1-52-7; 1-54-8; 1-80-1; 2-11-2: 2-12-14: 3-32-12; 3-34-1; 5-37-10; 6-21-2, 6-44-13, 8--6-1, 21, 31, 35; 8-13-6 : 8-14-5, 11 ; 8-82-27; 8-47-8 ; 10-50-4 ; 12-120-5).**NOTE**

To give exceeding power to the valour stimulated by that Soma-drinking, the gods and men place the thunderbolt in Indra's hand (1-63-2; 2-20-8; 3-32-12).

Moreover, the other gods also establish all their strength in Indra himself. (1-80-15 : 6-20-2 ; 10-48-3 ; 10-113-8 ; 10-120-3 ; 10-56-4 ; 8-15-8).

And they seat him in the chariot and make him ready for battle. (1-55-3 ; 1-130-1 ; 6-12-8).

It is described in many places that at the time of the slaying of the asura Vṛtra the Maruts give encouragement to this Indra and make his strength stand firm and bring victory. That makes the power of Soma-drinking still greater,

### Printed p. 83

(या अभजो मरुत इंद्र सोमे ये त्वामवर्धन्नभवन् गणस्ते (*yā abhajo maruta iṃdra some ye tvāmavardhannabhavan gaṇaste*)).

(3-35-9 ; 3.32-4; 3-47.3 ; 6.7-11 ; 8-7.24 ; 10-73-1, 2; 10-113-3).

In this context it is necessary to state the natural relationship and companionship between Indra and the Maruts. This section shows Indra's regard for Soma. But since it also indicates how stimulating that Soma-drinking is for the slaying of Vṛtra and what the role of the Maruts is in this, this subject too is incidental.

In 1-165 the dispute between Indra and the Maruts is described. In 1-165-8 Indra boasts that he slew Vṛtra by his own strength alone, without the help of the Maruts. In 7-21-6 the same praise is made by a devotee of Indra. (स्वेना हि वृत्रं शवसा जघंथ (*svenā hi vṛtraṃ śavasā jaghaṃtha*)) In the same way in 10-138-6 also he is praised with एता त्या ते श्रुत्यानि केवला यदेकः (*etā tyā te śrutyāni kevalā yadekaḥ*) and so on. It is stated that because of this prowess all the gods fear Indra. (5-30-5). In 8-7-1 it is asked whether it was not a fault that the Maruts did not help their friend Indra in a time of distress, and how, on such an occasion, they can be worthy of trust.

In 4-18-11 Aditi, the mother of Indra, grieved that all the other gods had abandoned her son without helping him, approaches Viṣṇu and prays that he help her son. But elsewhere there is a somewhat different account. It is stated that when he was fighting the asura Vṛtra, all the other gods, frightened by his terrible form, fled leaving Indra alone; and that Indra, being a friend of the Maruts, destroyed Vṛtra with their help. (8-96-7). Sāyaṇa has given the purport on this occasion so that there is no contradiction with the earlier sentences. And he has supported his meaning by quoting a sentence of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa. The sentence of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa is as follows.—

इंद्रो वै वृत्रं हनिष्यन्सर्वा देवता अब्रवीदनु मोपतिष्ठध्वमुप मा ह्वयध्वमिति तथेति तं हनिष्यंत आद्रवन्सोऽवेन्मां वै हनिष्यंत आद्रवंति हंतेमान्फीषया इति तानभिप्राश्वसीत्तस्य श्वसथादीषमाणा विश्वे देव अद्रवन् मरुतो हैनं नाजहुः प्रहर भगवो जहि वीरयस्वेत्येवैनमेतां वाचं वदंत उपातिष्ठंत । (*iṃdro vai vṛtraṃ haniṣyansarvā devatā abravīdanu mopatiṣṭhadhvamupa mā hvayadhvamiti tatheti taṃ haniṣyaṃta ādravanso 'venmāṃ vai haniṣyaṃta ādravaṃti haṃtemānphīṣayā iti tānabhiprāśvasīttasya śvasathādīṣamāṇā viśve deva adravan maruto hainaṃ nājahuḥ prahara bhagavo jahi vīrayasvetyevainametāṃ vācaṃ vadaṃta upātiṣṭhaṃta |*) तदेतदृषिः पश्यन्नभ्यनूनाच वृत्रस्य त्वा श्वसथादीषमाणा विश्वे देवा अजहुर्ये सखायः । (*tadetadṛṣiḥ paśyannabhyanūnāca vṛtrasya tvā śvasathādīṣamāṇā viśve devā ajahurye sakhāyaḥ |*) मरुद्भिरिंद्र सख्यं ते (*marudbhiriṃdra sakhyaṃ te*)

### Printed p. 84

थेमा विश्वाः पृतना जयासीति (*themā viśvāḥ pṛtanā jayāsīti*) सोऽवेदिमे वै किल मे सचिना इमे माकामायंत हंतेमानस्मिन्मुक्त आभजा इति ॥ (*so 'vedime vai kila me sacinā ime mākāmāyaṃta haṃtemānasminmukta ābhajā iti ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 3-20)

Indra, wishing to kill the asura Vṛtra, said to the gods, "O gods, follow me without leaving me and be my helpers." Then they gave the assurance, "We shall do so," and rushed forward to kill Vṛtra. Then Vṛtra, saying, "These gods are rushing forward to kill me; I shall frighten them," breathed a mighty breath and made a terrible sound. Then all the gods, hearing that terrible sound, were afraid and ran in all directions. But the Maruts alone did not forsake Indra. Standing beside Indra they gave him encouragement, saying, "O Indra, strike him. Show your prowess." Praising the friendship of those Maruts, the seer recited the mantra beginning वृत्रस्य त्वा (*vṛtrasya tvā*) and praised them (8-96-7). Then Indra said, "These Maruts alone are my true friends. They love me; therefore in the uktha mantra offered to me I shall give them also a share."

In the Ṛk Saṃhitā, in mantra 10-32-14, describing this same occasion, he is praised with अहेर्यातारं (*aheryātāraṃ*). Here too the meaning is doubtful. It is stated here that even Indra had a little fear at the time of the asura Vṛtra. Out of that fear, running like a hawk, he crossed the ninety-nine river-streams that he met on the way and escaped. In this way two or three kinds of purport can be given. But in the present case this discussion is unnecessary. The other gods who helped Indra are also praised in some places. For example—

"O Aśvins, lords of radiance, having drunk the sweet Soma with Indra, you helped him when he was destroying the asura Namuci, and at that time you protected him." (10-131-4)

"O Indra, as parents protect their children, the two Aśvins protected Indra in a time of danger. When you drank Soma at the time of the manifestation of your valour, the goddess Sarasvatī, with all her means of protection, was a helper at your side." (10-13-5)

This episode is told also in the Vājasaneya Saṃhitā (10-33, 34). And in the commentary on the Vājasaneya Saṃhitā the commentator has quoted a story told in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa. Its form is as follows—

### Printed p. 85

इंद्रस्येंद्रियमन्नस्य रसग्‌ंसोमस्य भक्षग्सुरयासुरो नमुचिरहरत्योऽश्विन् च सरस्वतीं चोपाधावछेपानोऽस्मि नमुचये न त्वा दिवा न नक्तग्ं हनानि न दण्णेन न धन्वना न पृथेन न मुष्टिना न शुष्केण नार्द्रेणाथ म इद महार्षिदिदं म आजहीर्षथेति । (*iṃdrasyeṃdriyamannasya rasagṃsomasya bhakṣagsurayāsuro namuciraharatyo 'śvin ca sarasvatīṃ copādhāvachepāno 'smi namucaye na tvā divā na naktagṃ hanāni na daṇṇena na dhanvanā na pṛthena na muṣṭinā na śuṣkeṇa nārdreṇātha ma ida mahārṣididaṃ ma ājahīrṣatheti |*)

(Śa. Brā. 12-7-3-1)

The asura Namuci once carried off Indra's valour, his food, his Soma and his liquor. Then Indra went to the Aśvins and to Sarasvatī and said, "I have given an assurance that I shall not kill Namuci either by day or by night. Not only this, I have also given the assurance that I shall not kill him with wood, or with my hand, or with the fist, or with a dry instrument, or with a wet instrument. Because of this assurance he has carried off my strength. Bring it back to me." They answered him in this way:

अस्तु नोऽत्राप्यथाहरामेति सह न एतदथाहरतेत्यब्रवीदिति । (*astu no 'trāpyathāharāmeti saha na etadathāharatetyabravīditi |*)

(12-7-3-2)

They said that it would do if a share were given to them also in that valour and in the Soma and the rest. Indra agreed to it.

तावश्विनी च सरस्वती च अपां फेनं वज्रमसिंचन्न शुष्को नार्द्र इति तेनेंद्रो नमुचेरासुरस्य व्युष्टायाग्ं रात्रावनुदित आदित्ये न दिवा न नक्तमिति शिर उदवासयत् (*tāvaśvinī ca sarasvatī ca apāṃ phenaṃ vajramasiṃcanna śuṣko nārdra iti teneṃdro namucerāsurasya vyuṣṭāyāgṃ rātrāvanudita āditye na divā na naktamiti śira udavāsayat*)

(12-7-3-3)

Then the Aśvins and Sarasvatī smeared the thunderbolt with foam. Then it was neither wet nor dry. With it Indra struck Namuci. Because he struck at dawn, when the night had passed and the sun had not yet risen, it was neither day nor night. With it he cut off Namuci's head.

तस्मादेतदृषिणाभ्यनूक्तं . . . . तस्य शीर्षंश्णिन्ने लोहितमिश्रः सोमोऽतिष्ठत्तस्मादबीभत्संत त एतदंधसोऽर्विपानमपश्यन्सो मो राज्ञामृतग्ं सुत इति तेनैनग्ं स्वदयित्वात्मन्नदधत ॥ ( (*tasmādetadṛṣiṇābhyanūktaṃ . . . . tasya śīrṣaṃśṇinne lohitamiśraḥ somo 'tiṣṭhattasmādabībhatsaṃta ta etadaṃdhaso 'rvipānamapaśyanso mo rājñāmṛtagṃ suta iti tenainagṃ svadayitvātmannadadhata ‖ (*) (12-7-3-4)

As soon as Namuci's head was split, the Soma became mixed with blood. Then it became loathsome. Then they, understanding that "King Soma, as soon as he is sprinkled, becomes immortal", poured that in with this Soma and drank it, so the description runs.

### Printed p. 86

But in this last part, the question whether "this Soma" and "that Soma" make two Somas deserves examination. For the present it is not relevant.

In the same way, in another context in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa it is stated that when Indra's power was diminished, the Aśvins, the physicians of the gods, and Sarasvatī caused his seat to be established again.

(12-7-1980 : 2-8-31)**NOTE**

Just as with the help of the Maruts Indra destroyed the power of the asuras, so also with the help of Bṛhaspati Indra destroyed the enemies of the gods who opposed him.

(7-85-15)

Thus, **NOTE** and stimulated by Soma-drinking, Indra goes to fight against Vṛtra, sometimes accompanied by the Maruts and sometimes by Viṣṇu.

(2-15 : 209.2 ; 6-47-1, 2 ; 10-32-1)**NOTE**

**The reason Indra is called Ṛjīṣī**

यजिषी (*yajiṣī*)—Yāska has given this etymology of this word—**NOTE**

यजिषी सोमो यत्सोमस्य पूयमानस्यातिरिच्यते तदृजेषमपार्जितं भवति तेनर्जिषी सोमः । अथाप्यैंद्रो निगमो भवति ऋजिषी वब्रे इति ॥ (*yajiṣī somo yatsomasya pūyamānasyātiricyate tadṛjeṣamapārjitaṃ bhavati tenarjiṣī somaḥ | athāpyaiṃdro nigamo bhavati ṛjiṣī vabre iti ‖*)

(Ni. 5-12)

The sapless, husk-like substance that remains after the Soma plant has been crushed with stones and the juice taken out is called ṛjīṣa. This ṛjīṣa is thrown away as useless. On some occasions such ṛjīṣa is soaked in water and a little more juice is squeezed out; such Soma juice is called ऋजीषः सोमः (*ṛjīṣaḥ somaḥ*). At the third pressing this ṛjīṣa Soma is mixed with flour and other āśira substances and offered to Indra. Because Indra drinks this ṛjīṣa Soma, it has become the custom to call Indra by the very word ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*). Moreover, Indra's horses, called हरी (*harī*), are famed as eating the husks of these Soma plants called ṛjīṣa. In this matter Yāska has given as an example the Vedic sentence बद्धां ते हरीधाना उप ऋजीषं जिघ्रतां (*baddhāṃ te harīdhānā upa ṛjīṣaṃ jighratāṃ*). The meaning of the above sentence is: O Indra, let your horses eat grain and smell this ṛjīṣa.

### Printed p. 87

What is ṛjīṣa? What is the reason for its having this name? What is its use in the sacrifice? We shall examine closely here what is said on these matters in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa and the Taittirīya Saṃhitā.

सोमो वै राजमुष्मिंल्लोक आसीत्तं देवाश्च ऋषयश्चाभ्यध्यायन् कथमयमस्मात्सोमो राजागच्छेदिति तेऽब्रुवंशृंदांसि यूयं न इमं सोमं राजानमाहरेति तथेति ते सुपर्णा भूत्वोदपतन् । (*somo vai rājamuṣmiṃlloka āsīttaṃ devāśca ṛṣayaścābhyadhyāyan kathamayamasmātsomo rājāgacchediti te 'bruvaṃśṛṃdāṃsi yūyaṃ na imaṃ somaṃ rājānamāhareti tatheti te suparṇā bhūtvodapatan |*)

(Ai. Brā. 13-25)

Formerly King Soma (the Soma plant; it is the custom to call the Soma plant King Soma as a mark of respect), that is, the Soma plant, was in the world of heaven. It was not in this world. Knowing this, the gods and the seers considered how to bring this Soma here. Then, having deliberated on this matter, and knowing that the metres had the power to bring Soma from heaven to the earth, they addressed the metres and prayed: "O metres, for our sake, that is, for use in our sacrifice, bring to the earth the Soma that is in heaven." Accepting their prayer, the metres took the forms of certain birds well known in the world and flew to the world of heaven. First the Jagatī metre took the form of a bird and flew towards the world of heaven. But before it had gone far it grew tired, could not go as far as heaven, and turned back. Then the Triṣṭubh metre said that it would bring the Soma and leapt into the atmosphere. But before it had gone a little beyond half the way it was worn out with weariness and turned back. When the gods saw that the Jagatī and Triṣṭubh metres had thus returned, unable to bring the Soma, they went to the Gāyatrī metre and prayed: "O Gāyatrī, bring Soma for us from heaven." Gāyatrī agreed that it should be so, took the form of a hawk, flew to heaven, frightened the guardians of Soma there, Sāna, Bhrāja and others, snatched the Soma and brought it to the earth. Because it brought the Soma plant, Gāyatrī obtained the foremost place among the metres.

सा यद्दक्षिणेन पदा समगृभ्नात्तत्प्रातःसवनमभवत्तद्गायत्री स्वमायतनम कुरुत तस्मात्तत्समृद्धतमं मन्यंते सर्वॆषां सवनानामग्रियो मुख्यो भवति श्रेष्ठतामश्नुते य एवं वेदाथ (*sā yaddakṣiṇena padā samagṛbhnāttatprātaḥsavanamabhavattadgāyatrī svamāyatanama kuruta tasmāttatsamṛddhatamaṃ manyaṃte sarvèṣāṃ savanānāmagriyo mukhyo bhavati śreṣṭhatāmaśnute ya evaṃ vedātha*) यत्सव्येन पदा समगृभ्नात्तन्माध्यंदिनं सवनमभवत्तद्विस्स्रंसत : तद्विस्स्रस्तं नान्वाप्पोत्तूर्वं सवनं ते देवाः प्राजिज्ञासंत तस्मिंस्किष्टुभं छंदसामदधुरिंद्रं देवतानां तेन (*yatsavyena padā samagṛbhnāttanmādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamabhavattadvissraṃsata : tadvissrastaṃ nānvāppottūrvaṃ savanaṃ te devāḥ prājijñāsaṃta tasmiṃskiṣṭubhaṃ chaṃdasāmadadhuriṃdraṃ devatānāṃ tena*)

### Printed p. 88

र्याभ्यां समावज्जामिभ्यां राध्योति य एवं वेदाथ यन्नुखेन समगृभ्णात्तत्तृतीयसवनमभवत् । (*ryābhyāṃ samāvajjāmibhyāṃ rādhyoti ya evaṃ vedātha yannukhena samagṛbhṇāttattṛtīyasavanamabhavat |*)

(Ai. Brā. 13-26)

When bringing the Soma plants, the hawk carried them, gripping them with both its feet and with its beak. The Soma (or Soma plant) that the bird brought holding it with its right foot became the morning pressing. Gāyatrī made it, that is, the morning pressing, her own place. Therefore among all the pressings the morning pressing is held by the sacrificial authorities to be the best of all. In that morning-pressing rite the application of mantras and the rest is also special. Therefore the man who knows this matter well is regarded as becoming the best and foremost among his equals. The portion of Soma that Gāyatrī brought holding it with her left foot became famed as the midday pressing. Because, when carrying the Soma plant with the left foot, the left foot did not have as much strength as the right, the plants kept slipping a little from the foot from time to time. For this reason this midday pressing did not become equal to the morning pressing in strength and capacity. Seeing this, the gods deliberated and established for the midday-pressing rite the Triṣṭubh metre and Indra, the best among the gods. Through the companionship of Indra, the best of the gods, and of the Triṣṭubh metre, dear to him, the midday pressing obtained a place equal to the morning pressing in strength, capacity and excellence. The man who knows this equality of the morning and midday pressings, and their strength, capacity and excellence, obtains prosperity in wealth and the rest, and well-being, and lives happily. Then the portion of Soma that Gāyatrī, in the form of the hawk, carried gripped in her beak became famed as the third pressing.

तस्य पतंती रसमधयत्तद्वीतरसं नान्वाप्पोत्पूर्वे सवने ते देवाः प्राजिज्ञासंत तत्पशुष्पपश्यंस्तद्यदाशिरमवनयंत्याज्येन पशुना चरंति तेन तत्समावद्वीर्यमभवत्पूर्वाभ्यां सवनाभ्यां सर्वैः सवनैः समावद्वीर्यैः समावज्जामिभी राध्योति य एवं वेद ॥ (*tasya pataṃtī rasamadhayattadvītarasaṃ nānvāppotpūrve savane te devāḥ prājijñāsaṃta tatpaśuṣpapaśyaṃstadyadāśiramavanayaṃtyājyena paśunā caraṃti tena tatsamāvadvīryamabhavatpūrvābhyāṃ savanābhyāṃ sarvaiḥ savanaiḥ samāvadvīryaiḥ samāvajjāmibhī rādhyoti ya evaṃ veda ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 13-27.)

While Gāyatrī (the hawk) was coming with the Soma plant gripped in her beak, she kept drinking the juice in the plant, sipping it little by little. Therefore, that is, because the Soma plants brought in the beak were not abundant in juice but poor in it, this third pressing did not become equal to the morning and midday pressings in strength, capacity and the rest. Therefore the gods, seeing this, deliberated and found that the means suited to it lay in cattle. From cattle come milk and curds, and from these the ghee (clarified butter) and other oblation materials are obtained. If milk is mixed with the Soma juice that is deficient in the plants, that juice becomes strong. If oblations and the like are made with ghee and with the parts of the animals, the pressing rite becomes complete. Therefore those who know the sacrificial rite, by adding milk and other āśira substances to the ṛjīṣa used in the third pressing (the husks of the Soma plant that remain after the juice has been taken at the morning and midday pressings are called ṛjīṣa), and by performing oblations with the ghee obtained from cattle and with the heart and other parts of the animals, understand that the third-pressing rite becomes equal to the morning and midday pressings in strength, capacity and the rest, and act accordingly. This matter is stated in another śākhā in this manner—

### Printed p. 89

ब्रह्मवादिनो वदंति कस्मात्सत्याद्दायत्री कनिष्ठा छंदसाग्ं सती यज्ञमुखं परियायेति यदेवादः सोममाहरत्तस्माद्यज्ञमुखं पर्यॆत्तस्मात्तेजस्वीनीतमा पद्भ्यां द्वे सवने समगृह्णान्मुखेनैकं यन्मुखेन समगृह्णात्तदधयत्तस्माद्वे सवने शुक्रवती प्रातःसवनं च माध्यंदिनं च तस्मात्तृतीयसवन ऋजीषमभिषुण्वंति धीतमिव हि मन्यंते अशिरमवन यति सशुक्रत्वाय ॥ (*brahmavādino vadaṃti kasmātsatyāddāyatrī kaniṣṭhā chaṃdasāgṃ satī yajñamukhaṃ pariyāyeti yadevādaḥ somamāharattasmādyajñamukhaṃ paryèttasmāttejasvīnītamā padbhyāṃ dve savane samagṛhṇānmukhenaikaṃ yanmukhena samagṛhṇāttadadhayattasmādve savane śukravatī prātaḥsavanaṃ ca mādhyaṃdinaṃ ca tasmāttṛtīyasavana ṛjīṣamabhiṣuṇvaṃti dhītamiva hi manyaṃte aśiramavana yati saśukratvāya ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-1-6-4)
