Thus the same purport is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. The man who knows this matter, that is, that the three pressings, the morning, the midday and the third, have obtained an equal place in strength, capacity and excellence, becomes possessed of all prosperity and lives happily.

**The view that Vāyu is entitled to drink Soma first among the gods**

In the Ṛk अग्रं पिब मधूनां (*agraṃ piba madhūnāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-46-1) it is said that Vāyu is entitled to drink Soma before Indra and the other gods. In support of this—

आ वायो भूष शुचिपा उप नः सहस्रं ते नियुतो विश्ववार । उपो ते अंधो मध्यमयामि यस्य देव दधिषे पूर्वपेयं ॥ (*ā vāyo bhūṣa śucipā upa naḥ sahasraṃ te niyuto viśvavāra | upo te aṃdho madhyamayāmi yasya deva dadhiṣe pūrvapeyaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-92-1)

quoting this Ṛk, it is indicated that there the seer prays that Vāyu should drink Soma first. There is an ancient account in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa of how Vāyu obtained the foremost place among the gods in the drinking of Soma; we explain it here—

### Printed p. 90

देवा वै सोमस्य राज्योऽग्रपेये न समपादयन्नहं प्रथमः पिबेयमहं प्रथमः पिबेयमित्येवाकामयंत ते संपादयंतोऽब्रुवन् हंताजिमयाम स यो न उज्जेष्यति स प्रथमः सोमस्य पास्यतीति तथेति त आजिमयुस्तेषामाजिं यथामभिसृष्टानां वायुर्मुखं प्रथमः प्रत्यपद्यताथेंद्रोऽथ मित्रावरुणावथाश्विनौ । (*devā vai somasya rājyo 'grapeye na samapādayannahaṃ prathamaḥ pibeyamahaṃ prathamaḥ pibeyamityevākāmayaṃta te saṃpādayaṃto 'bruvan haṃtājimayāma sa yo na ujjeṣyati sa prathamaḥ somasya pāsyatīti tatheti ta ājimayusteṣāmājiṃ yathāmabhisṛṣṭānāṃ vāyurmukhaṃ prathamaḥ pratyapadyatātheṃdro 'tha mitrāvaruṇāvathāśvinau |*)

(Ai. Brā. 9-25)

Formerly all the gods, desiring to drink Soma together, fell into a dispute as to who among them should drink Soma first; each began to argue, "I first, I should drink first." Since the matter could not be settled in any way, they all gathered, took counsel, and came to an agreement: "Let us all arrange a contest; whoever wins in it shall be entitled to drink Soma first." When all agreed to this, the contest was fixed thus: running from one place to another place far away, whoever first reached a rock or a tree there should be regarded as the winner. When all consented to the agreement that the one who thus won should drink Soma as the victor among them, all the gods began to run at the same moment. Among all the running gods Vāyu reached the appointed place before all the others. Indra came just behind Vāyu and became second. After Indra came Mitra and Varuṇa, and after them the Aśvins ran up and reached the appointed place. Therefore among these gods Vāyu ran ahead of all and became the victor.

सोऽवेदिंद्रो वायुमुद्वै जयतीति तमनु परापतत्सह नावथोज्जयावेति स नेत्यब्रवीदहमेवोज्जॆष्यामीति तृतीयं मेऽथोज्जयावेति नेति हैवाब्रवीदहमेवोज्जॆष्यामीति तुरीयं मेऽथोज्जयावेति तथेति तं तुरीयेऽत्यार्जत तत्तुरीयभागिंद्रोऽभवत्रिभाग्वायुः ॥ (*so 'vediṃdro vāyumudvai jayatīti tamanu parāpatatsaha nāvathojjayāveti sa netyabravīdahamevojjèṣyāmīti tṛtīyaṃ me 'thojjayāveti neti haivābravīdahamevojjèṣyāmīti turīyaṃ me 'thojjayāveti tatheti taṃ turīye 'tyārjata tatturīyabhāgiṃdro 'bhavatribhāgvāyuḥ ‖*)

While Vāyu was running and Indra behind him, this conversation took place between them. Indra, seeing Vāyu running swiftly ahead of him, resolved, "This Vāyu alone will run ahead of all and win. Somehow I too must run with him and win," and using all his strength he ran close beside Vāyu. Yet there was no sign that he could leave Vāyu behind and go ahead; so Indra said to Vāyu: "O Vāyu, we two are ahead of everyone. Therefore let us regard both of us as having won and share the first Soma-drinking between us. Half for me, half for you. Let both of us

### Printed p. 91

co-operate and gain the victory." Hearing that, Vāyu said: "O Indra, I am ahead of you, if only by a little. Therefore I alone shall be the victor. In the first Soma-drinking everything must be mine. I cannot give you half." Then Indra said: "Very well, then I do not want half. Of three parts take two yourself. Give me only one part." But Vāyu did not agree even to that, saying: "In any case I shall win. The whole share will be mine; I do not wish to give you even one part out of three." Then Indra prayed to Vāyu again: "I do not want even one part out of three. Even less, that is, if you give me one part out of four it is enough. Agree at least to that." Vāyu agreed to that, saying, "Let it be so." Therefore it was arranged that in the Aindravāyava graha (that is, when Indra and Vāyu drink Soma together) Indra should have one part and Vāyu three parts.

तौ सहैवेंद्रनायू उदजयतां सह मित्रावरुण् सहाश्विन् त एषामेते यतोज्जितं भक्षा इंद्रवाय्वोः प्रथमोऽथ मित्रावरुणयोरथाश्विनोः. स एष इंद्रतुरियो ग्रहो गृह्यते यद्यैंद्रवायवः । (*tau sahaiveṃdranāyū udajayatāṃ saha mitrāvaruṇ sahāśvin ta eṣāmete yatojjitaṃ bhakṣā iṃdravāyvoḥ prathamo 'tha mitrāvaruṇayorathāśvinoḥ. sa eṣa iṃdraturiyo graho gṛhyate yadyaiṃdravāyavaḥ |*)

Thus Indra and Vāyu, co-operating, won the contest and reached the appointed place first. Behind them Mitra and Varuṇa came and arrived. Behind them the Aśvins came. Therefore among these gods they became entitled to Soma-drinking in the order of the places they took in the contest. That is, it was arranged that first Indra and Vāyu, afterwards Mitra and Varuṇa, and after them the Aśvins should drink Soma. Thus the custom of two gods drinking Soma together) came into practice. Therefore in the Aindravāyava graha Indra has only one part out of four in the Soma-drinking.

तदेतदृषिः पश्यन्नभ्यनूनाच नियुत्वा इंद्रसारथिरिति (*tadetadṛṣiḥ paśyannabhyanūnāca niyutvā iṃdrasārathiriti*)

The seer, knowing by divine vision that Indra has one part out of four in the Aindravāyava graha, expressed this matter in the next Ṛk, that is, in शतेना नो अभिष्टिभिर्नियुत्वा इंद्रसारथिः (*śatenā no abhiṣṭibhirniyutvā iṃdrasārathiḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-46.2), by the phrase नियुत्वा इंद्रसारथिः (*niyutvā iṃdrasārathiḥ*). Here, as in the Nirukta statement नियुतो वायोः (*niyuto vāyoḥ*) (Ni. 2-28), the horses called niyuta are famed as the vehicle of the god Vāyu. Therefore the word niyutvat indicates relation to Vāyu. In the above Ṛk the word नियुत्वान् (*niyutvān*) indicates Indra accompanied by Vāyu. Mantras such as अधा वायुं नियुतः सश्चतस्वाः (*adhā vāyuṃ niyutaḥ saścatasvāḥ*) (Tai. Brā. 2-8-1-1) support this. The word इंद्रसारथिः (*iṃdrasārathiḥ*) means that Indra is the charioteer of Vāyu. Since Indra

### Printed p. 92

stands in the position of charioteer to Vāyu, it is indicated by this that Indra has only a fourth part in the first Soma-drinking.

तस्माद्वाप्येतर्हि भरताः सत्वनां नित्तिं प्रयंति तुरिये हैव संग्रहीतारो वदंतेऽमुनैवानूकाशेन यदद इंद्रः सारथिरिव भूत्वो दजयत् ॥ (*tasmādvāpyetarhi bharatāḥ satvanāṃ nittiṃ prayaṃti turiye haiva saṃgrahītāro vadaṃte 'munaivānūkāśena yadada iṃdraḥ sārathiriva bhūtvo dajayat ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 9-25)

The matter stated above is made clear by an example from the practice of the world. Warriors in battle, seated in chariots, with the help of their charioteers become victorious in the fight and seize the wealth and other possessions of their enemies. When they have thus seized it, they give one part of that wealth and the rest, that is, one part out of four, to the charioteers who helped them. This custom is established in worldly practice. In the same way, in this contest arranged by the gods, Indra became the charioteer of Vāyu and Vāyu became the victor; so a fourth part of the first Soma-drinking won in the contest became Indra's share. The main purport is that Indra obtained his fourth part by being charioteer to Vāyu.

**How the Ṛbhus obtained the right to drink Soma**

Sāyaṇa has told the ancient story relating to this in the introduction to his commentary on the Ṛk 1-161-1. The three sons of Sudhanvan, Ṛbhu, Vibhvan and Vāja, though men, obtained divinity by their good deeds, and once, at the time of a sacrifice, set out with the gods to drink Soma. Agni, urged on by the gods, seeing them of the same form, took on the same form himself and began to drink with them. Not knowing who the fourth one was who had come with them in their own shape, they doubt among themselves. In the first half of this Ṛk that inquiry is stated, and in the second half it is stated that after some time they determine who he is and, conversing with him, tell him of the greatness of the cup (camasa).

The noble birth of these brothers, the greatness of their good deeds, the trouble the gods gave them out of jealousy when they joined the gods, and the help that Prajāpati and Savitṛ gave them at that time are also told in some places. For example—

यभवो वै देवेषु तपसा सोमपीथमभ्यजयंस्तेभ्यः प्रातस्सवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तानग्निर्वसुभिः प्रातःसवनादनुदत तेभ्यो माध्यंदिने सवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तानिंद्रो रुद्रॆर्माध्यंदिनात्सवनादनुदत (*yabhavo vai deveṣu tapasā somapīthamabhyajayaṃstebhyaḥ prātassavane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstānagnirvasubhiḥ prātaḥsavanādanudata tebhyo mādhyaṃdine savane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstāniṃdro rudrèrmādhyaṃdinātsavanādanudata*) तेभ्यस्तृतीय (*tebhyastṛtīya*)

### Printed p. 93

सवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तान्विश्वे देवे अनोनुद्यंत नेह पास्यंति नेहेति स प्रजापतिरब्रवीत्सवितारं तव ना इमेंतेवासास्तमेवैभिः संपिबस्वीति स तथेत्यब्रवीत्सविता तान्वै त्वमुभयतः परिपिबेति तान्प्रजापतिरुभयतः पर्यपिबत् ॥ (*savane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstānviśve deve anonudyaṃta neha pāsyaṃti neheti sa prajāpatirabravītsavitāraṃ tava nā imeṃtevāsāstamevaibhiḥ saṃpibasvīti sa tathetyabravītsavitā tānvai tvamubhayataḥ paripibeti tānprajāpatirubhayataḥ paryapibat ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 3-3-30)

By their austerity and their intense desire the Ṛbhus obtained among the gods the right to drink Soma. When they first wished to obtain it at the morning pressing, Agni, joining with the Vasus, rejected them and drove them away. Then, when they wished to obtain it at the midday pressing, Indra, joining with the Rudras, drove them away. Again, when they wished to obtain it at the third pressing, the Viśvedevas rejected them there too with harsh words: "You are certainly not entitled to drink Soma here. You shall not do so." Then Prajāpati said to Savitṛ: "These Ṛbhus are your pupils. Drink Soma with them." Savitṛ agreed to that. Prajāpati also, staying at their sides, drank the Soma juice. In this way the story is told in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa.

**The seven Soma sacrifice forms**

There are seven varieties of the Soma sacrifice. It is the custom to call them the seven Soma-saṃsthās. They are named Agniṣṭoma, Atyagniṣṭoma, Ukthya, Ṣoḍaśī, Atirātra, Apporyāma and Vājapeya.**NOTE** Among these the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice is of the nature of the prakṛti, that is, this sacrifice is the basis for the other Soma sacrifices. With some modifications it becomes the remaining six Soma sacrifices.

**The fruit of the Soma sacrifice**

In the Ṛk 1-140-12 the (Soma) sacrifice is compared to a boat, worldly existence to the ocean, and the priests to the oars. Poverty, fear of enemies and other kinds of sorrow are the terrible situations of the ocean of worldly existence. To cross this ocean the boat is the chief means, but for this boat to carry the sacrificer safely across the ocean it is not possible without the boat and its oars. Therefore the prayer is made that the boat of the Soma sacrifice, propelled by the oars in the form of excellent priests, may carry us safely across the ocean of worldly existence.

### Printed p. 94

In general, in the Ṛk-saṃhitā, to compare sorrow such as poverty, its cause sin, and the enemy to a deep flood of water, and the help of the gods to a boat, is a most fitting simile. For example:

जातवेदसे सुनवाम सोममरातीयतो नि दहाति वेदः । स नः पर्षदति दुर्गाणि विश्वा नावेव सिंधुं दुरितात्यग्निः ॥ (*jātavedase sunavāma somamarātīyato ni dahāti vedaḥ | sa naḥ parṣadati durgāṇi viśvā nāveva siṃdhuṃ duritātyagniḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-99-1)

नू न इंद्रावरुणा गृणाना पृंक्तं रयिं सौश्रवसाय देवा । इत्था गृणंतो महिनस्य शर्धोऽपो न नावा दुरितं तरेम ॥ (*nū na iṃdrāvaruṇā gṛṇānā pṛṃktaṃ rayiṃ sauśravasāya devā | itthā gṛṇaṃto mahinasya śardho 'po na nāvā duritaṃ tarema ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-8-8)

Just as in these and similar ṛks it is prayed that, as one crosses a river easily by a boat, so one may cross over sin with the help of the deities such as Agni, so here too Agni is prayed to: provide us with a boat in the form of the sacrifice.

Here the benefit of the boat in the form of the Soma sacrifice is said to be of three kinds: first, to make the sacrificers and their sons wealthy; second, to carry the sacrificers across worldly existence; third, to bestow the happiness of the supremely pure world of Brahmā. Sāyaṇa has given the explanation for the phrases अस्माकं वीरान् मघोनः (*asmākaṃ vīrān maghonaḥ*); उत नः जनांश्च पारयात् (*uta naḥ janāṃśca pārayāt*); च या शर्म नावं (*ca yā śarma nāvaṃ*). Not only in the Ṛk-saṃhitā but also in the Śatapatha and other Brāhmaṇas this same simile is well known. For example:

नौर्ह ना एष्या स्वर्ग्या । यद्बहिष्पवमानं तस्याः ऋत्विज एव स्फाश्चारित्राश्च स्वर्गस्य लोकस्या संसारणास्तस्या एक एव मज्जयिताय एव निंद्यः स यथा पूर्णामभ्यारुह्य मज्जयेदेवं हैनां समज्जयति तद्वै सर्व एव यज्ञो नौः स्वर्ग्या तस्मादु सर्वस्मादेव यज्ञान्निंद्यं परिबिबाधिषेत । (*naurha nā eṣyā svargyā | yadbahiṣpavamānaṃ tasyāḥ ṛtvija eva sphāścāritrāśca svargasya lokasyā saṃsāraṇāstasyā eka eva majjayitāya eva niṃdyaḥ sa yathā pūrṇāmabhyāruhya majjayedevaṃ haināṃ samajjayati tadvai sarva eva yajño nauḥ svargyā tasmādu sarvasmādeva yajñānniṃdyaṃ paribibādhiṣeta |*) (Śa. Brā. 4-2-5-10)

The Soma sacrifice, and the Bahiṣpavamāna stotra that is to be recited on the first day of the Soma sacrifice, are boats that lead one to heaven. The priests themselves are their oars. If any one of these priests follows a blameworthy way of life, he makes these boats sink. Therefore such a man must be kept away from the sacrificial act. So in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa; and likewise:

यज्ञो वै सुशर्मा नः (*yajño vai suśarmā naḥ*) (Ai. Brā. 1-13)**NOTE**

### Printed p. 95

नावमिहारुहेमेति समेवैनमेतदधिरोहति स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य समष्टेलि संपत्तैलि संगत्यैलि॥ (*nāvamihāruhemeti samevainametadadhirohati svargasya lokasya samaṣṭeli saṃpattaili saṃgatyaili‖*) (Ai. Brā. 4-20)

Thus in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa too this kind of simile is well known. And because the fruit of this sacrifice is the highest, even the gods desire it and are devoted to the sacrifice. Therefore humans too, following them, should be devoted to the sacrificial work; so it teaches:

त आसत । श्रियं गच्छेम । यशः स्यामान्नादाः स्यामेति तथो एवेमे सत्त्रमासते श्रियं गच्छेम यशः सामान्यदाः स्यामेति । ते होचुः यो नः श्रमेण तपसा श्रद्धया यज्ञेनाहुतिभिर्यज्ञस्योदृचं पूर्वोवगच्छात्स नः श्रेष्ठोऽसत्तदु नः सर्वेषां सहेति तथेति । तद्विष्णुः प्रथमः प्राप । स देवानां श्रेष्ठोऽभवत्तस्मादाहुर्विष्णुदेवानां श्रेष्ठ इति । (*ta āsata | śriyaṃ gacchema | yaśaḥ syāmānnādāḥ syāmeti tatho eveme sattramāsate śriyaṃ gacchema yaśaḥ sāmānyadāḥ syāmeti | te hocuḥ yo naḥ śrameṇa tapasā śraddhayā yajñenāhutibhiryajñasyodṛcaṃ pūrvovagacchātsa naḥ śreṣṭho 'sattadu naḥ sarveṣāṃ saheti tatheti | tadviṣṇuḥ prathamaḥ prāpa | sa devānāṃ śreṣṭho 'bhavattasmādāhurviṣṇudevānāṃ śreṣṭha iti |*) (Śa. Brā. 14-1-1-2, 3)

Because through the performance of the sacrifice dignity, greatness and wealth of food are obtained, the gods desired them and became devoted to the sacrifice. Among them Viṣṇu obtained the first place, and therefore Viṣṇu became the best among the gods. Thus the fruit of the sacrifice is praised.

**Removal of various sins for those who have studied the Soma mantras**

Although the Nirukta and the Upaniṣads such as the Chāndogya say that the principal sinful acts are seven, there are in fact many kinds of sin. If a sinner studies regularly, from the mouth of a teacher, the 610 ṛks praising the deity Pavamāna Soma, that is, the sūktas 1-67 at the beginning of the ninth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, containing 610 ṛks in the Gāyatrī metre, then the Veda-puruṣa himself says in the Pariśiṣṭa mantras that he will remove the many kinds of sins of such a man and make him pure. They are explained here.

ऋषयस्तु तपस्तेपुः सर्वे स्वर्गजिगीषवः । तपंतस्तपसोग्रेण पावमानीर्यचोऽब्रुवन् (*ṛṣayastu tapastepuḥ sarve svargajigīṣavaḥ | tapaṃtastapasogreṇa pāvamānīryaco 'bruvan*) (Pariśiṣṭa. 18-6)**NOTE**

In ancient times all the seers, desiring heaven, performed fierce austerity, studied these Pāvamānī ṛks and praised them thus as removers of sin.

### Printed p. 96

यन्ने गर्भे वसतः पापमुग्रं यज्जायमानस्य च किंचिदन्यत् । जातस्य च यच्चापि च वर्धतो मे तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*yanne garbhe vasataḥ pāpamugraṃ yajjāyamānasya ca kiṃcidanyat | jātasya ca yaccāpi ca vardhato me tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Hari. 18-7)**NOTE**

The Veda-puruṣa, the Lord of all, has spoken thus: whatever grave sin a man has committed while dwelling in the womb, then at birth, and after birth while growing up, if he studies the Pāvamānī mantras I destroy all such sins and make him pure.

मातापित्रोर्यन्न कृतं वचो मे यत्स्थावरं जंगममाबभूव । विश्वस्य तत्पृह्रिहितं वचो मे तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*mātāpitroryanna kṛtaṃ vaco me yatsthāvaraṃ jaṃgamamābabhūva | viśvasya tatpṛhrihitaṃ vaco me tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Sari. 12-8)**NOTE**

I remove and make pure the sin of having transgressed the word of father and mother, and whatever sin arises in this world of the moving and the unmoving, for any living being, from words spoken wishing it harm.

गोघ्नात् तस्करत्वात् स्त्रीवधाद्यच्च किल्पिसं । पापकं च चरणेभ्यस्तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*goghnāt taskaratvāt strīvadhādyacca kilpisaṃ | pāpakaṃ ca caraṇebhyastatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Sari. 18-9)**NOTE**

I remove and make pure the sin that may arise from killing a cow, theft, killing a woman, and the deed of trampling and harming living beings with the feet.

ब्रह्मवधात्सुरापानात्स्वर्णस्तेयाद्वृषलिगमनमैथुनसंगमात् । गुरोर्दाराधिगमनाच्च तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*brahmavadhātsurāpānātsvarṇasteyādvṛṣaligamanamaithunasaṃgamāt | gurordārādhigamanācca tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-10)**NOTE**

I remove and make pure the sin of killing a Brāhmaṇa, drinking liquor, stealing gold, consorting with the woman called vṛṣalī (one who is unmarried although her younger sister is married), and of intercourse with the wife of one's teacher.

बालघ्नान्मातृपितृवधाद्भूमितस्करात्सर्ववर्णगमनमैथुनसंगमात् । पापेभ्यश्च प्रतिग्रहात्सद्यः प्रहरति सर्वदुष्कृतं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*bālaghnānmātṛpitṛvadhādbhūmitaskarātsarvavarṇagamanamaithunasaṃgamāt | pāpebhyaśca pratigrahātsadyaḥ praharati sarvaduṣkṛtaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Sari. 18-11)**NOTE**

I remove and make pure whatever sins arise from killing children, killing one's mother and father, fraudulently seizing land such as fields, consorting with women of all low castes, accepting an improper gift, and doing other evil deeds.

### Printed p. 97

क्रयविक्रयाद्योनिदोषाद्भक्षाद्योज्यात्पतिग्रहात् । असंभोजनाच्चापि नृशंसं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*krayavikrayādyonidoṣādbhakṣādyojyātpatigrahāt | asaṃbhojanāccāpi nṛśaṃsaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-12)**NOTE**

I remove the sins that arise from buying and selling in an unjust way, that is, fraudulently, from consorting with women one has come upon, from eating what may not be eaten, from accepting what may not be accepted, and from eating one's meal leaving aside a guest who has come to the house.

दुर्यष्टं दुरधीतं पापं यच्चाज्ञानतो कृतं । अयाजिताश्च संयाज्यास्तत्पानमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*duryaṣṭaṃ duradhītaṃ pāpaṃ yaccājñānato kṛtaṃ | ayājitāśca saṃyājyāstatpānamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Sari. 18-13)**NOTE**

I remove and make pure the sins that arise from such evil deeds as performing a sacrifice in some way other than that prescribed, studying the Veda without discipline, sin committed through ignorance, having a sacrifice performed for those who are not fit to perform it, and not having it performed for those who are fit for it.

अमंत्रमन्नं यत्किंचिद्दूयते च हुताशने । संवत्सरकृतं पापं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*amaṃtramannaṃ yatkiṃciddūyate ca hutāśane | saṃvatsarakṛtaṃ pāpaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Sari. 18-14)**NOTE**

I remove the sin that arises from offering oblation in the fire without the prescribed recitation of mantras, or while reciting the mantra wrongly, and whatever sins have been committed during the year, and make him pure.

ऋतस्य योनयोऽमृतस्य धाम विश्वा देवेभ्यः पुण्यगंधाः । ता न आपः प्र वहंतु पापं शुद्धा गच्छामि सुकृतामु लोकं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*ṛtasya yonayo 'mṛtasya dhāma viśvā devebhyaḥ puṇyagaṃdhāḥ | tā na āpaḥ pra vahaṃtu pāpaṃ śuddhā gacchāmi sukṛtāmu lokaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-15)**NOTE**

May the heavenly waters, which are the chief support of truth or of the sacrifice, which dwell in the eternal heaven, and which bring delight to the gods, wash away the sins mentioned above. Having made that man pure, I shall cause him to obtain the excellent world.

### Printed p. 98

**The seers of the ninth maṇḍala**

In this maṇḍala are the seers of other maṇḍalas themselves. Only the deity is Pavamāna Soma. The names of these seers and the number of mantras seen by them in this maṇḍala are given below.

Madhucchandā Vaiśvāmitra (10), Medhātithi Kāṇva (10), Śunaḥśepa Ājīgarti (11), Hiraṇyastūpa Āṅgirasa (10), Kāśyapa Asita or Devala (164), Dṛḷhacyuta Āgastya (6), Iddhavāha Dārthacyuta (6), Nṛmedha (12), Priyamedha (6), Bindu (6), Gotama (6), Śyāvāka (6), Trita (22), Prabhūvasu (12), Rahūgaṇa (12), Bṛhanmati (12), Medhyātithi (18), Ayāsya (15), Kavi Bhārgava (15), Ucathya (15), Avatsāra (32), Amahīyu (30), Jamadagni (30), Nighruvi Kāśyapa (30), Kaśyapa (57), Bhṛgu Vāruṇi or Jamadagni (30), Śata Vaikhānasa (30), the Saptarṣis (32), Vatsapri Bhālandana (10), Hiraṇyastūpa (11), Reṇu Vaiśvāmitra (10), Ṛṣabha Vaiśvāmitra (9), Harimanta (9), Pavitra (14), Kakṣīvān (9), Kavi (20), Vasu Bhāradvāja (15), Prajāpati Vācya (5), Vena Bhārgava (12), Ākṛṣṭāmāṣa (10), Sikatā Nivāvarī (10), Pṛśni and Ajā (10), the three Ṛṣigaṇas (10), Atri (5), Gṛtsamada (3), Uśanā (24), Vasiṣṭha (9), Nodhā (5), Kaṇva (5), Praskaṇva (5), Pratardana Daivodāsi (24), Indrapramati Vāsiṣṭha (3), Vṛṣagaṇa Vāsiṣṭha (3), Manyu Vāsiṣṭha (3), Upamanyu Vāsiṣṭha (3), Vyāghrapād Vāsiṣṭha (3), Śakti Vāsiṣṭha (3), Karṇaśrut Vāsiṣṭha (3), Mṛḷīka Vāsiṣṭha (3), Vasukra Vāsiṣṭha (3), Parāśara (14), Kutsa (14), Ambarīṣa and Ṛjiśvā (12), the two Kāśyapas Rebha and Sūnu (17), Andhīgu Śyāvāśvi (3), Yayāti Nāhuṣa (3), Nahuṣa Mānava (3), Manu Sāṃvaraṇa (3), Prajāpati (3), Dvita (6), Parvata and Nārada (12), Agni Cākṣuṣa (12), Cakṣu Mānava (3), Manu Āpsava (3), Agni (5), the Saptarṣis (26), Gauriviti (2), Śakti (4), Ūru (2), Ṛjiśvā (2), Ūrdhasadyā (2), Kṛtayaśā (2), Ṛṇañcaya (2), Agni, Dhiṣṭā and Aiśvara (22), Tryaruṇa and Trasadasyu (12), Anānata Pārucchepi (3), Śiśu (4).

In this maṇḍala there are 114 sūktas, 7 anuvākas and 1108 ṛks.

### Printed p. 99

## Sūkta 44 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ॥ श्रीगणेशाय नमः ॥ यस्य निःश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् ।
> निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरम् ॥
> अथ सप्तमाष्टकस्य प्रथमाध्याय आरभ्यते । ‘प्र णः' इति षडृचं विंशं सूक्तमांगिरसस्यायास्यस्यार्षं गायत्रं पवमानसोमदेवताकम् । तथा चानुक्रांतं- प्र णोऽयास्यः' इति । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa begins with the invocation "Salutation to Śrī Gaṇeśa" and a verse: him from whose breath the Vedas came, who out of the Vedas created the whole world, that Vidyātīrtha Maheśvara I praise. He then says that the first chapter of the seventh aṣṭaka now begins. The sūkta प्र णः (*pra ṇaḥ*) is a sūkta of six ṛks, the twentieth (in the anuvāka); its seer is Ayāsya Āṅgirasa, its metre Gāyatrī, its deity Pavamāna Soma. And so the Anukramaṇī says प्र णोऽयास्यः (*pra ṇo 'yāsyaḥ*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

Translation: The first chapter of the seventh aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā now begins. Since nineteen sūktas of the second anuvāka of the ninth maṇḍala were completed in the previous chapter, this sūkta that now begins here, प्र ण इंदो (*pra ṇa iṃdo*), is the twentieth sūkta in the second anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is आयास्य आंगिरसः (*āyāsya āṃgirasaḥ*); the deity is Pavamāna Soma; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said प्रणोऽयास्यः (*praṇo 'yāsyaḥ*). The viniyoga has been stated before.

Sūkta 44. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 44. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 1. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Ayāsya. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ण॑ इंदो म॒हे तन॑ ऊ॒र्मिं न बिभ्र॑दर्षसि ।  
> अ॒भि दे॒वाँ अ॒यास्यः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । नः । इंदो इति । महे । तने । ऊर्मिम् । न । बिभ्रत् । अर्षसि ।  
> अभि । देवान् । अयास्यः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 100

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंदो सोम त्वं नः अस्माकं महे महते तने धनाय प्र अर्षसि प्रगच्छसि । न संप्रति अयास्यः चायमृषिः तव ऊर्मिं तरंगं बिभ्रत् धारयन् देवान् अभि गच्छति यष्टुम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Soma: O Indu, you advance (अर्षसि (*arṣasi*), glossed as going forth) for our great wealth (he glosses महे (*mahe*) as "great" and तने (*tane*) as "for wealth"). He glosses न (*na*) as "now" (संप्रति (*saṃprati*)) and says that this seer Ayāsya, bearing your wave (ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*), glossed तरंगं (*taraṃgaṃ*)), goes towards the gods in order to sacrifice.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- महे (*mahe*)—great
- तने (*tane*)—for the acquiring of wealth
- प्र अर्षसि (*pra arṣasi*)—you advance
- अयास्यः (*ayāsyaḥ*)—the seer named Ayāsya
- ऊर्मि० न (*ūrmi0 na*)—your juices that are like waves
- बिभ्रत् (*bibhrat*)—bearing
- देवान् (*devān*)—to the gods
- अभि (*abhi*) (गच्छति (*gacchati*))—goes for worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, you advance for the acquiring of great wealth for us. The seer named Ayāsya (I), bearing your juices that are like waves, goes to the gods for the sake of the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Indu, you advance to give us abundant wealth; Ayasya bearing your waves (goes) towards the gods (to sacrifice).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒ती जु॒ष्टो धि॒या हि॒तः सोमो॑ हिन्वे परा॒वति॑ ।  
> विप्र॑स्य॒ धार॑या क॒विः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मती । जुष्टः । धिया । हितः । सोमः । हिन्वे । पराऽवति ।  
> विप्रस्य । धारया । कविः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कविः क्रांतकर्मा सोमः विप्रस्य मेधाविनः स्तोतुः मती मत्या स्तुत्या जुष्टः सेवितः धिया कर्मणा हितः यज्ञे निहितः परावति पवित्राद्दूरदेशे धारया हिन्वे प्रेर्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Soma, कविः (*kaviḥ*), is "of far-reaching action" (क्रांतकर्मा (*krāṃtakarmā*)). He is served (जुष्टः (*juṣṭaḥ*)) by the praise (मत्या स्तुत्या (*matyā stutyā*)) of the wise (मेधाविनः (*medhāvinaḥ*)) worshipper, and is placed (हितः (*hitaḥ*)) in the sacrifice by the rite (धिया कर्मणा (*dhiyā karmaṇā*)). In a place far from the filter (पवित्राद्दूरदेशे (*pavitrāddūradeśe*), glossing परावति (*parāvati*)) he is sent (हिन्वे प्रेर्यते (*hinve preryate*)) in a stream (धारया (*dhārayā*)).

### Printed p. 101

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कविः (*kaviḥ*)—one of far-reaching action
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—Soma-deva
- विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)—of the wise praiser
- मती (*matī*)—by praise
- जुष्टः (*juṣṭaḥ*)—having been served
- धिया (*dhiyā*)—by an excellent rite
- हितः (*hitaḥ*)—having been established in the sacrifice
- परावति (*parāvati*)—to a very distant place
- धारया (*dhārayā*)—in the form of a stream
- हिन्वे (*hinve*)—is sent

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Soma-deva, of far-reaching action, served by the praise of the wise praiser and established in the sacrifice by an excellent rite, is sent in the form of a stream to a very distant place (to the sacrificial altar).

##### English translation
The sage Soma gratified by the praise of the pious (worshipper) prepared for the sacrifice is sent in a stream at a distance (from the filter).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं दे॒वेषु॒ जागृ॑विः सु॒त ए॑ति प॒वित्र॒ आ ।  
> सोमो॑ याति॒ विच॑र्षणिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयम् । देवेषु । जागृविः । सुतः । एति । पवित्रे । आ ।  
> सोमः । याति । विऽचर्षणिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> जागृविः जागरणशीलः अयं सोमः देवेषु देवार्थं सुतः अभिषुतः आ एति समंतात् गच्छति । अपि च विचर्षणिः विद्रष्टा सोमः पवित्रे याति पावनाय गच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma, जागृविः (*jāgṛviḥ*), is "given to waking" (जागरणशीलः (*jāgaraṇaśīlaḥ*)). Pressed (सुतः (*sutaḥ*)) for the gods (देवार्थं (*devārthaṃ*)), he goes all around (समंतात् गच्छति (*samaṃtāt gacchati*), glossing आ एति (*ā eti*)). Further, Soma as विचर्षणिः (*vicarṣaṇiḥ*), "the one who sees everything" (विद्रष्टा (*vidraṣṭā*)), goes (याति (*yāti*)) to the filter (पवित्रे (*pavitre*)) for the sake of purification (पावनाय (*pāvanāya*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जागृविः (*jāgṛviḥ*)—ever vigilant
- अयं सोमः (*ayaṃ somaḥ*)—this Soma-deva
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—for the worship of the gods
- सुतः (*sutaḥ*)—pressed
- आ एति (*ā eti*)—moves around

### Printed p. 102

- विचर्षणिः (*vicarṣaṇiḥ*)—Soma, who has a sight that examines everywhere
- पवित्रे (*pavitre*)—towards the दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*)
- याति (*yāti*)—goes for purification

॥ भास्कर (*bhāskara*) ॥**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This ever-vigilant Soma-deva, pressed for the worship of the gods, flows all around. Soma, who has a sight that examines everywhere, goes to the दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*) for purification.

##### English translation
This vigilant soma effused for the gods approaches, all-beholding he goes to the filter.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नः॑ पवस्व वाज॒युश्च॑क्रा॒णश्चारु॑मध्व॒रम् ।  
> ब॒र्हिष्माँ॒ आ वि॑वासति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नः । पवस्व । वाजऽयुः । चक्राणः । चारुम् । अध्वरम् ।  
> बर्हिष्मान् । आ । विवासति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम यं त्वां बर्हिष्मान् ऋत्विक् आ विवासति परिचरति सः त्वं नः अस्मदर्थं वाजयुः अन्नमिच्छन् 'अध्वरं हिंसारहितं यागं चारु कल्याणं चक्राणः कुर्वन् पवस्व क्षर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, the priest who has the sacrificial grass (बर्हिष्मान् ऋत्विक् (*barhiṣmān ṛtvik*)) serves (परिचरति (*paricarati*), glossing आ विवासति (*ā vivāsati*)) you. You, desiring food for us (वाजयुः अन्नमिच्छन् (*vājayuḥ annamicchan*)), making the non-violent sacrifice (अध्वरं हिंसारहितं यागं (*adhvaraṃ hiṃsārahitaṃ yāgaṃ*)) auspicious (चारु कल्याणं (*cāru kalyāṇaṃ*)), flow (पवस्व क्षर (*pavasva kṣara*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) बर्हिष्मान् (*barhiṣmān*)—the priest who is ready, having prepared the seat of darbha grass
- आ विवासति (*ā vivāsati*)—who serves you
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a you
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वाजयुः (*vājayuḥ*)—desiring food
- अध्वरं (*adhvaraṃ*)—the non-violent sacrifice
- चारुं (*cāruṃ*)—as auspicious
- चक्राणः (*cakrāṇaḥ*)—making
- पवस्व (*pavasva*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, you whom the priest, ready after preparing the seat of darbha grass, serves, flow desiring food for us and making our non-violent sacrifice auspicious.

### Printed p. 103

##### English translation
Flow for us food-desiring, making (our) sacrifice auspicious; (O Soma, whom) the priest with the sacred grass adores.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नो॒ भगा॑य वा॒यवे॒ विप्र॑वीरः स॒दावृ॑धः ।  
> सोमो॑ दे॒वेष्वा य॑मत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नः । भगाय । वायवे । विप्रऽवीरः । सदाऽवृधः ।  
> सोमः । देवेषु । आ । यमत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सः पवमानः सोमः वायवे वायुदेवार्थं भगाय भगदेवार्थं च विप्रवीरः विप्रैर्मेधाविभिः स्तुत्या प्रेरितः सदावृधः नित्यवृद्धो भवन् नः अस्मभ्यं देवेषु स्थितं धनम् आ यमत् आ प्रयच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Pavamāna Soma, विप्रवीरः (*vipravīraḥ*), impelled by praise (स्तुत्या प्रेरितः (*stutyā preritaḥ*)) by the wise priests (विप्रैर्मेधाविभिः (*viprairmedhāvibhiḥ*)), ever growing (सदावृधः नित्यवृद्धो भवन् (*sadāvṛdhaḥ nityavṛddho bhavan*)), for Vāyu (वायुदेवार्थं (*vāyudevārthaṃ*)) and for Bhaga (भगदेवार्थं (*bhagadevārthaṃ*)), may bestow (आ यमत् आ प्रयच्छतु (*ā yamat ā prayacchatu*)) on us the wealth that is placed among the gods (देवेषु स्थितं धनम् (*deveṣu sthitaṃ dhanam*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वायवे (*vāyave*)—for the god Vāyu
- भगाय (*bhagāya*)—for the god Bhaga
- विप्रवीरः (*vipravīraḥ*)—one impelled by wise praisers
- सः सोमः (*saḥ somaḥ*)—that Soma-deva, who is also pure
- सदावृधः (*sadāvṛdhaḥ*)—ever attaining growth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—the wealth that is established among the gods
- आ यमत् (*ā yamat*)—may he bring and supply

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Soma-deva, for the god Vāyu and for the god Bhaga, impelled by wise praisers and pure, ever attaining growth, bring and supply to us the wealth established among the gods.

##### English translation
May soma who is pressed forth by the seers for Bhaga and Vayu, ever prospering, grant us (wealth placed) amongst the gods.

### Printed p. 104

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नो॑ अ॒द्य वसु॑त्तये क्रतु॒विद्गा॑तु॒वित्त॑मः ।  
> वाजं॑ जेषि॒ श्रवो॑ बृ॒हत् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नः । अद्य । वसुत्तये । क्रतुऽवित् । गातुवित्ऽतमः ।  
> वाजम् । जेषि । श्रवः । बृहत् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम क्रतुवित् क्रतूनां कर्मणां लंभकः गातुवित्तमः पुण्यलोकानामतिशयेन मार्गस्य ज्ञाता त्वम् अद्य अस्मिन्नहनि नः अस्माकं वसुत्तये धनलाभाय बृहत् महत् श्रवः अन्नं वाजं बलं च जेषि जय ॥ ॥ १ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, क्रतुवित् (*kratuvit*) is "obtainer of the rites" (क्रतूनां कर्मणां लंभकः (*kratūnāṃ karmaṇāṃ laṃbhakaḥ*)), and गातुवित्तमः (*gātuvittamaḥ*) is "in the highest degree the knower of the way to the meritorious worlds" (पुण्यलोकानामतिशयेन मार्गस्य ज्ञाता (*puṇyalokānāmatiśayena mārgasya jñātā*)). You, today, on this day, for our gain of wealth (वसुत्तये धनलाभाय (*vasuttaye dhanalābhāya*)), win (जेषि जय (*jeṣi jaya*)) great (बृहत् महत् (*bṛhat mahat*)) food (श्रवः अन्नं (*śravaḥ annaṃ*)) and strength (वाजं बलं च (*vājaṃ balaṃ ca*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) क्रतुवित् (*kratuvit*)—receiver of sacrificial rites
- गातुमित्तमः (*gātumittamaḥ*)—one who knows the good paths in the highest degree
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this sacrifice day
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वसुत्तये (*vasuttaye*)—for the gain of abundant wealth
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—food
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—strength
- जेषि (*jeṣi*)—acquire and grant

**NOTE**

### Printed p. 106

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, receiver of sacrificial rites and knower in the highest degree of the good paths to the meritorious worlds, you, on this sacrifice day, acquire and grant to us abundant food and strength for the gain of abundant wealth.

##### English translation
Receiver of sacrifices, knower of (pious) paths, grant us this day abundant food and strength for the acquisition of wealth.

## Sūkta 45 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘स पवस्व' इति षडृचमेकविंशं सूक्तम् । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । ‘स पवस्व ' इत्यनुक्रांतम् । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta स पवस्व (*sa pavasva*) has six ṛks and is the twenty-first sūkta. The seer and the rest are as before (पूर्ववत् (*pūrvavat*)). The Anukramaṇī says स पवस्व (*sa pavasva*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

Translation: This sūkta स पवस्व (*sa pavasva*) is the twenty-first sūkta in the second anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as of the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said स पवस्व (*sa pavasva*). The viniyoga has been stated before.

Sūkta 45. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 45. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Ayāsya. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स प॑वस्व॒ मदा॑य॒ कं नृ॒चक्षा॑ दे॒ववी॑तये ।  
> इंद॒विंद्रा॑य पी॒तये॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । पवस्व । मदाय । कम् । नृऽचक्षाः । देवऽवीतये ।  
> इंदो इति । इंद्राय । पीतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंदो सोम नृचक्षाः नॄणां नेतॄणां द्रष्टा सः त्वं देववीतये यज्ञाय इंद्राय इंद्रस्य पीतये पानाय च मदाय मदार्थं च कं सुखं यथा भवति तथा पवस्व क्षर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indu, Soma, you who are नृचक्षाः (*nṛcakṣāḥ*), "the beholder of the men, the leaders" (नॄणां नेतॄणां द्रष्टा (*nṝṇāṃ netṝṇāṃ draṣṭā*)): flow (पवस्व क्षर (*pavasva kṣara*)) for the sacrifice (देववीतये यज्ञाय (*devavītaye yajñāya*)), for Indra's drinking (इंद्रस्य पीतये पानाय (*iṃdrasya pītaye pānāya*)) and for his exhilaration (मदाय मदार्थं (*madāya madārthaṃ*)), in such a way that there is happiness (कं सुखं यथा भवति तथा (*kaṃ sukhaṃ yathā bhavati tathā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma-deva
- नृचक्षाः (*nṛcakṣāḥ*)—one who has a sight that looks upon the deeds of men
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- देववीतये (*devavītaye*)—for the sacrifice, the means of worshipping the gods
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—of Indra
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking
- मदाय (*madāya*)—for his delight
- कं (*kaṃ*)—so that happiness is obtained by him
- पवस्व (*pavasva*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, you who have a sight that looks upon the deeds of men, flow for the sacrifice that is the means of worshipping the gods, for Indra's drinking and for his delight, so that happiness is obtained by him.

##### English translation
Indu, the beholder of men, flow pleasantly for the banquet of the gods, for Indra's drinking and exhilaration.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नो॑ अर्षा॒भि दू॒त्यं१॒॑ त्वमिंद्रा॑य तोशसे ।  
> दे॒वान्सखि॑भ्य॒ आ वर॑म् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नः । अर्ष । अभि । दूत्यम् । त्वम् । इंद्राय । तोशसे ।  
> देवान् । सखिऽभ्यः । आ । वरम् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम त्वं नः अस्माकं दूत्यं दूतस्य कर्म अर्ष अभिगच्छ । अपि च यस्त्वं इंद्राय इंद्रार्थं तोशसे पीयसे सः त्वं सखिभ्यः प्रियेभ्यः अस्मभ्यं वरं श्रेष्ठं धनं देवान् आ याचस्वेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, you, for us, go to (अभिगच्छ (*abhigaccha*), glossing अर्ष (*arṣa*)) the messenger's office (दूत्यं दूतस्य कर्म (*dūtyaṃ dūtasya karma*)). Further, you who are drunk (पीयसे (*pīyase*), glossing तोशसे (*tośase*)) for Indra: from the gods (देवान् (*devān*)) ask (आ याचस्व (*ā yācasva*)) for us, your dear friends (सखिभ्यः प्रियेभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ priyebhyaḥ*)), the excellent wealth (वरं श्रेष्ठं धनं (*varaṃ śreṣṭhaṃ dhanaṃ*)). Such is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- दूत्यं (*dūtyaṃ*)—the messenger's office
- अभि अर्ष (*abhi arṣa*)—go and undertake
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's satisfaction
- तोशसे (*tośase*)—you who are drunk
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a you
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—for us, your friends
- वरं (*varaṃ*)—the most excellent wealth
- देवान् (*devān*)—from the gods
- आ (*ā*) (याचस्व (*yācasva*))—ask

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, for us undertake the messenger's office of going to and returning from the gods. You who are drunk for Indra's satisfaction, ask the gods for the most excellent wealth for us, your friends.

### Printed p. 107

##### English translation
Approach the office of messenger for us: you (who) are drunk for Indra, (pour) on the gods wealth for (us their) friends.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त त्वाम॑रु॒णं व॒यं गोभि॑रंज्मो॒ मदा॑य॒ कम् ।  
> वि नो॑ रा॒ये दुरो॑ वृधि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । त्वाम् । अरुणम् । वयम् । गोभिः । अंज्मः । मदाय । कम् ।  
> वि । नः । राये । दुरः । वृधि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उत अपि च हे सोम यम् अरुणम् अरुणवर्णं त्वां मदाय मदार्थं वयम् अंगिरसायास्याः गोभिः गोविकारैः पयोभिः अंज्मः वासयामः संस्कुर्मः। कम् इति पूरणम् । स त्वं नः अस्माकं राये धनाय दुरः द्वाराणि वि वृधि विवृतानि कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Moreover (उत अपि च (*uta api ca*)), O Soma, we, the people of Aṅgirasa Ayāsya (अंगिरसायास्याः (*aṃgirasāyāsyāḥ*))**NOTE**, make ready (वासयामः संस्कुर्मः (*vāsayāmaḥ saṃskurmaḥ*), glossing अंज्मः (*aṃjmaḥ*)) you, the ruddy one (अरुणम् अरुणवर्णं (*aruṇam aruṇavarṇaṃ*)), for exhilaration (मदार्थं (*madārthaṃ*)), with the products of the cow, that is, with milk (गोविकारैः पयोभिः (*govikāraiḥ payobhiḥ*)). कम् (*kam*) is a filler (पूरणम् (*pūraṇam*)). You, for our wealth (राये धनाय (*rāye dhanāya*)), open (विवृतानि कुरु (*vivṛtāni kuru*)) the doors (दुरः द्वाराणि (*duraḥ dvārāṇi*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) उत (*uta*)—and
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- अरुणम् (*aruṇam*)—golden-hued
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—whom, you
- मदाय (*madāya*)—for the delight of Indra and others
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—mixing with milk and other supporting substances
- अंज्मः (*aṃjmaḥ*)—we prepare; such a you
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- राये (*rāye*)—leading to wealth
- दुरः (*duraḥ*)—the doors
- वि वृधि (*vi vṛdhi*)—open fully

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, we prepare you, of attractive golden hue, mixing you with milk and other supporting substances for the delight of Indra and others. Such a you, open fully for us the way that provides wealth and the doors that lead to wealth.

##### English translation
And we adorn you, the purple-tinted, with milk and curds for the purpose of exhilaration, open the doors for our riches.

### Printed p. 108

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अत्यू॑ प॒वित्र॑मक्रमीद्वा॒जी धुरं॒ न याम॑नि ।  
> इंदु॑र्दे॒वेषु॑ पत्यते ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अति । ऊं इति । पवित्रम् । अक्रमीत् । वाजी । धुरम् । न । यामनि ।  
> इंदुः । देवेषु । पत्यते ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंदुः सोमः वाजी अश्वः यामनि गमने धुरं न रथस्य धुरं यथा तथा पवित्रम् अति अक्रमीत् अतिगच्छति । देवेषु देवानां मध्ये पत्यते गच्छति च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indu, Soma, passes (अति अक्रमीत् अतिगच्छति (*ati akramīt atigacchati*)) the filter (पवित्रम् (*pavitram*)) as the horse (वाजी अश्वः (*vājī aśvaḥ*)), in going (यामनि गमने (*yāmani gamane*)), goes to the yoke-pole of the chariot (रथस्य धुरं (*rathasya dhuraṃ*)). And he goes (गच्छति (*gacchati*), glossing पत्यते (*patyate*)) among the gods (देवानां मध्ये (*devānāṃ madhye*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यामनि (*yāmani*)—at the time of moving
- वाजी (*vājī*)—the horse
- धुरं न (*dhuraṃ na*)—as it enters between the yoke-poles of the chariot
- इंदुः (*iṃduḥ*)—Soma-deva
- पवित्रं (*pavitraṃ*)—the purifying दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*)
- अति अक्रमीत् (*ati akramīt*)—enters for purification
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—in the midst of the gods
- पत्यते (*patyate*)—goes and enters

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a horse, at the time of moving, enters between the yoke-poles of the chariot, Soma-deva enters the purifying दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*) for purification. He also goes and enters into the midst of the gods.

##### English translation
Indu passes the filter as a horse in going passes the shaft (of the chariot); he proceeds to the midst of the gods.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समी॒ सखा॑यो अस्वर॒न्वने॒ क्रीळं॑त॒मत्य॑विम् ।  
> इंदुं॑ ना॒वा अ॑नूषत ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 109

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सम् । ईमिति । सखायः । अस्वरन् । वने । क्रीळंतम् । अतिऽअविम् ।  
> इंदुम् । नावाः । अनूषत ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्यविम् अतिक्रांतं दशापवित्रं वने उदके क्रीडंतं संक्रीडमानम् ईम् एनम् इंदुं सोमं सखायः प्रियस्तोतारः सम् अस्वरन् संस्तुवंति । नावाः वाचोऽपि अनूषत अस्तुवन् । ‘ नौरक्षरम्' इति वाङ्नामसु पाठात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The friends, the dear praisers (सखायः प्रियस्तोतारः (*sakhāyaḥ priyastotāraḥ*)), praise together (सम् अस्वरन् संस्तुवंति (*sam asvaran saṃstuvaṃti*)) this Indu, Soma (एनम् इंदुं सोमं (*enam iṃduṃ somaṃ*)), who has passed the ten-threaded filter (अत्यविम् अतिक्रांतं दशापवित्रं (*atyavim atikrāṃtaṃ daśāpavitraṃ*)) and sports (क्रीडंतं संक्रीडमानम् (*krīḍaṃtaṃ saṃkrīḍamānam*)) in the water (वने उदके (*vane udake*)). The नावाः (*nāvāḥ*), that is, the speeches (वाचोऽपि (*vāco 'pi*)), also praised (अनूषत अस्तुवन् (*anūṣata astuvan*)), because in the list of names of speech there is the reading नौरक्षरम् (*naurakṣaram*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अत्यविम् (*atyavim*)—after passing the दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*) and attaining purity
- वने (*vane*)—joining the water
- क्रीडंतं (*krīḍaṃtaṃ*)—sporting
- ईम् (*īm*) इंदुं (*iṃduṃ*)—this Soma-deva
- सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*)—his friends, the praisers
- सम् अस्वरन् (*sam asvaran*)—gather together and praise
- नावाः (*nāvāḥ*)—their words of praise also
- अनूषत (*anūṣata*)—praise him

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This Soma-deva, who after passing the दशापवित्र (*daśāpavitra*) and attaining purity joins the water and sports, is praised by his friends, the praisers, who gather together. Their words of praise also extol him.

##### English translation
His friends praise Indu sporting in the water and passing through the fleece; their hymns glorify him.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तया॑ पवस्व॒ धार॑या॒ यया॑ पी॒तो वि॒चक्ष॑से ।  
> इंदो॑ स्तो॒त्रे सु॒वीर्य॑म् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तया । पवस्व । धारया । यया । पीतः । विऽचक्षसे ।  
> इंदो इति । स्तोत्रे । सुऽवीर्यम् ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 110

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंदो त्वं यया धारया पीतः सन् विचक्षसे विचक्षणाय स्तोत्रे स्तोत्राणां कर्त्रे पुरुषाय सुवीर्यं शोभनवीर्यं प्रयच्छसीति शेषः। तया धारया पवस्व क्षर ॥ ॥ २ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indu, with the stream (धारया (*dhārayā*)) by which you, when drunk (पीतः सन् (*pītaḥ san*)), bestow (प्रयच्छसीति शेषः (*prayacchasīti śeṣaḥ*), the verb being supplied) good vigour (सुवीर्यं शोभनवीर्यं (*suvīryaṃ śobhanavīryaṃ*)) on the discerning (विचक्षणाय (*vicakṣaṇāya*)) man who makes praises (स्तोत्राणां कर्त्रे पुरुषाय (*stotrāṇāṃ kartre puruṣāya*)), with that stream flow (पवस्व क्षर (*pavasva kṣara*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma-deva
- यया (*yayā*)—by which
- धारया (*dhārayā*)—in the form of a stream
- पीतः (*pītaḥ*)—having been drunk
- विचक्षसे (*vicakṣase*)—to the one of subtle sight
- स्तोत्रे (*stotre*)—to the praiser
- सुवीर्यम् (*suvīryam*)—you become the giver of excellent vigour
- तया (*tayā*)—by such a stream
- पवस्व (*pavasva*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, flow in that stream by which, when drunk, you become the giver of excellent vigour to the praiser of subtle sight.

##### English translation
Flow, Indu, with that stream wherewith when drunk you bestow much vigour on your discerning worshipper.

## Sūkta 46 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘ असृग्रन्' इति षडृ चं द्वाविंशं सूक्तम्। ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् ।  असृग्रन्' इत्यनुक्रांतम् । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta असृग्रन् (*asṛgran*) has six ṛks and is the twenty-second sūkta. The seer and the rest are as before (पूर्ववत् (*pūrvavat*)). The Anukramaṇī says असृग्रन् (*asṛgran*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

Translation: This sūkta असृग्रन् (*asṛgran*) is the twenty-second sūkta in the second anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as of the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said असृग्रन् (*asṛgran*). The viniyoga has been stated before.

Sūkta 46. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 46. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 3. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Ayāsya. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

### Printed p. 111

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> असृ॑ग्रंदे॒ववी॑त॒येऽत्या॑सः॒ कृत्व्या॑ इव ।  
> क्षरं॑तः पर्वता॒वृधः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> असृग्रन् । देवऽवीतये । अत्यासः । कृत्व्याःऽइव ।  
> क्षरंतः । पर्वतऽवृधः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पर्वतावृधः पर्वतैरभिषवग्रावभिर्वृद्धाः पर्वतेषु जाता वा क्षरंतः सोमाः देववीतये यज्ञाय अत्यासः अश्वाः कृत्व्याइव यथा कर्मण्या अश्वाः तद्वत् असृग्रन् सृज्यंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Soma juices (सोमाः (*somāḥ*)), पर्वतावृधः (*parvatāvṛdhaḥ*), grown by the mountains, that is, by the pressing stones (पर्वतैरभिषवग्रावभिर्वृद्धाः (*parvatairabhiṣavagrāvabhirvṛddhāḥ*)), or born on the mountains (पर्वतेषु जाता वा (*parvateṣu jātā vā*)), flowing (क्षरंतः (*kṣaraṃtaḥ*)), are sent forth (असृग्रन् सृज्यंते (*asṛgran sṛjyaṃte*)) for the sacrifice (देववीतये यज्ञाय (*devavītaye yajñāya*)), as horses fit for work (कर्मण्या अश्वाः (*karmaṇyā aśvāḥ*)) are.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कृत्व्या (*kṛtvyā*)—skilled in work
- अत्यासः (*atyāsaḥ*)—like horses
- पर्वतावृधः (*parvatāvṛdhaḥ*)—grown in mountain regions, or increased by the pressing stones
- क्षरंतः (*kṣaraṃtaḥ*)—flowing
- देववीतये (*devavītaye*)—for the drinking of the gods
- असृग्रन् (*asṛgran*)—are created

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Like horses skilled in work, the Soma juices, grown in mountain regions or increased by the pressing stones, flowing, are created for the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Begotten by the stones the flowing (Soma-juices) are effused for the banquet of the gods like active horses.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> परि॑ष्कृतास॒ इंद॑वो॒ योषे॑व॒ पित्र्या॑वती ।  
> वा॒युं सोमा॑ असृक्षत ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 112

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परिऽकृतासः । इंदवः । योषाऽइव । पित्र्यऽवती ।  
> वायुम् । सोमाः । असृक्षत ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंदवः यागेषु क्लिद्यमानाः सोमाः परिष्कृतासः परिष्कृता अलंकृताः संतः पित्र्यावती पितृमती योषेव अलंकृता कन्यका यथा वरं प्रति गच्छति तद्वत् वायुं प्रति असृक्षत गच्छंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Somas (इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)), being moistened in the sacrifices (यागेषु क्लिद्यमानाः (*yāgeṣu klidyamānāḥ*)), adorned (परिष्कृता अलंकृताः (*pariṣkṛtā alaṃkṛtāḥ*)), go (गच्छंति (*gacchaṃti*), glossing असृक्षत (*asṛkṣata*)) towards Vāyu (वायुं प्रति (*vāyuṃ prati*)), as an adorned maiden who has a father (पित्र्यावती पितृमती योषेव अलंकृता कन्यका (*pitryāvatī pitṛmatī yoṣeva alaṃkṛtā kanyakā*)) goes to the bridegroom (वरं प्रति (*varaṃ prati*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पित्र्यावती (*pitryāvatī*)—one who has a loving father
- योषेव (*yoṣeva*)—as a maiden adorns herself well
- इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)—pressed for the sacrifice
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—the Soma juices
- परिष्कृतासः (*pariṣkṛtāsaḥ*)—well adorned
- वायुं (*vāyuṃ*)—towards Vāyu
- असृक्षत (*asṛkṣata*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a maiden who has a loving father adorns herself in the best manner, the Soma juices pressed for the sacrifice, well adorned, flow towards Vāyu.

##### English translation
The Soma-juices pressed (at the sacrifice) adorned like a bride who has a father flow to Vayu.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ते सोमा॑स॒ इंद॑वः॒ प्रय॑स्वंतश्च॒मू सु॒ताः ।  
> इंद्रं॑ वर्धंति॒ कर्म॑भिः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एते । सोमासः । इंदवः । प्रयस्वंतः । चमू इति । सुताः ।  
> इंद्रम् । वर्धंति । कर्मऽभिः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 113

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंदवः दीप्ताः प्रयस्वंतः अन्नवंतः एते अस्मिन् कर्मणि वर्तमाना अमी सोमासः सोमाः चमू चम्वोरधिषवणफलकयोः सुताः अभिषुताः संतः कर्मभिः यागैः इंद्रं वर्धयंति प्रीणयंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains: the Soma-juices (सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*)) that are present in this rite are radiant (इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)) and provided with food (प्रयस्वंतः (*prayasvaṃtaḥ*)). They are pressed on the two pressing-boards (चमू (*camū*), understood as the two boards used for pressing), and by the sacrificial rites (कर्मभिः (*karmabhiḥ*), that is, by sacrifices) they magnify Indra, that is, they gratify him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)—endowed with lustre
- प्रयस्वंतः (*prayasvaṃtaḥ*)—endowed with food
- एते (*ete*)—appointed in this rite
- सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*)—the Soma-juices
- चमू (*camū*)—by the pressing boards
- सुताः (*sutāḥ*)—pressed
- कर्मभिः (*karmabhiḥ*)—by the sacrificial rites
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- वर्धंति (*vardhaṃti*)—they gratify

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Soma-juices, endowed with lustre, endowed with food, appointed in the present rite, are pressed on the pressing boards and gratify Indra by the sacrificial rites.

##### English translation
These brilliant Soma-Juices, bestowing pleasant food, expressed into
the vessel, gratify Indra with the ceremonies.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ धा॑वता सुहस्त्यः शु॒क्रा गृ॑भ्णीत मं॒थिना॑ ।  
> गोभिः॑ श्रीणीत मत्स॒रम् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । धावत । सुऽहस्त्यः । शुक्रा । गृभ्णीत । मंथिना ।  
> गोभिः । श्रीणीत । मत्सरम् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सुहस्त्यः शोभनहस्ता ऋत्विजः आ धावत । मां संप्रति आगच्छत । मंथिना सह शुक्रा शुक्रं च गृभ्णीत गृह्णीत सोमम् । मत्सरं सोमं गोभिः गोविकारैः पयोभिः श्रीणीत संस्कुरुत च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O priests of good hands (सुहस्त्यः (*suhastyaḥ*)), run hither, that is, come to me. Take up the pure Soma (शुक्रा (*śukrā*)) together with the churning-vessel (मंथिना (*maṃthinā*)). Mix and prepare the exhilarating Soma (मत्सरं (*matsaraṃ*)) with the products of the cow (गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)), that is, with milk.

### Printed p. 114

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुहस्त्यः (*suhastyaḥ*)—O priests of excellent hands
- आ धावत (*ā dhāvata*)—run hither
- मंथिना (*maṃthinā*)—with the ladle **NOTE**
- शुक्रा (*śukrā*)—the radiant Soma
- गृभ्णीत (*gṛbhṇīta*)—receive
- मत्सरं (*matsaraṃ*)—the exhilarating Soma
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with milk and the other accompanying substances
- श्रीणीत (*śrīṇīta*)—mix and prepare

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests of excellent hands, run hither. Receive with the ladle the radiant Soma. Mix the exhilarating Soma with milk and the other accompanying substances, and prepare it.

##### English translation
Dexterous (priests) hasten (to me), take with the ladle the pure (Soma) mix the exhilarating (Soma) with milk and curds.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स प॑वस्व धनंजय प्रयं॒ता राध॑सो म॒हः ।  
> अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ सोम गातु॒वित् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । पवस्व । धनम्ऽजय । प्रऽयंता । राधसः । महः ।  
> अस्मभ्यम् । सोम । गातुऽवित् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे धनंजय शत्रुसंबंधिनां जनानां जेतः सोम गातुवित् अभीष्टमार्गस्य लंभकः अस्मभ्यं महः महतः राधसः धनस्य प्रयंता प्रदाता च यः सः त्वं पवस्व क्षर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, conqueror (धनंजय (*dhanaṃjaya*)) of the people belonging to the enemies, finder (गातुवित् (*gātuvit*)) of the desired path, and giver (प्रयंता (*prayaṃtā*)) to us of great wealth (राधसः (*rādhasaḥ*)): you who are such, flow, that is, stream. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- धनंजय (*dhanaṃjaya*)—able to win the wealth of enemies
- सोम (*soma*)—O divine Soma
- गातुवित् (*gātuvit*)—knower of good paths
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—great
- राधसः (*rādhasaḥ*)—of wealth
- प्रयंता (*prayaṃtā*)—the excellent giver
- सः (*saḥ*)—the renowned one
- पवस्व (*pavasva*)—flow, purifying

### Printed p. 115

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, able to win the wealth of enemies, knower of good paths, excellent giver to us of great wealth, renowned one, flow, purifying.

##### English translation
Soma, conqueror of wealth, who know the way (to fulfil my desires), flow forth the bestower of vast wealth upon us.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒तं मृ॑जंति॒ मर्ज्यं॒ पव॑मानं॒ दश॒ क्षिपः॑ ।  
> इंद्रा॑य मत्स॒रं मद॑म् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एतम् । मृजंति । मर्ज्यम् । पवमानम् । दश । क्षिपः ।  
> इंद्राय । मत्सरम् । मदम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मर्ज्यं मर्जनीयं शोध्यं पवमानं क्षरंतं मत्सरं मदकरम् एतम् इमं मदं सोमं दशसंख्याकाः क्षिपः अंगुलयः । ‘ व्रिशः क्षिपः' इत्यंगुलिनामसु पाठात् । इंद्रार्थं मृजंति पवित्रे शोधयंति ॥ ॥ ३ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma (मदं (*madaṃ*)) is fit to be cleansed (मर्ज्यं (*marjyaṃ*)), that is, to be purified; it flows (पवमानं (*pavamānaṃ*)) and is exhilarating (मत्सरं (*matsaraṃ*)), that is, it produces intoxication. The ten (दश (*daśa*)) क्षिपः (*kṣipaḥ*), that is, the fingers, cleanse it for Indra in the filter. Sāyaṇa supports this with the reading व्रिशः क्षिपः (*vriśaḥ kṣipaḥ*), found among the names of the fingers.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मर्ज्यं (*marjyaṃ*)—fit to be cleansed
- पवमानं (*pavamānaṃ*)—flowing
- मत्सरं (*matsaraṃ*)—intoxicating
- एतं मदं (*etaṃ madaṃ*)—this Soma
- दश क्षिपः (*daśa kṣipaḥ*)—the ten fingers
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- मृजंति (*mṛjaṃti*)—press in the ten-fold filter

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The ten fingers press in the ten-fold filter, for Indra's drinking, this Soma who is fit to be cleansed, who flows and who intoxicates.

### Printed p. 116

##### English translation
The ten fingers purtfy this purifiable pure flowing exhilarating juice for Indra.

## Sūkta 47 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘अया सोमः' इति पंचर्चं त्रयोविंशं सूक्तं भृगुपुत्रस्य कवेरार्षं गायत्रं पवमानसोमदेवताकम् । तथा चानुक्रांतम् -- ‘अया सोमः पंच कविर्भार्गवः' इति । उक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning अया सोमः (*ayā somaḥ*) has five ṛks and is the twenty-third sūkta. Its seer is Kavi, the son of Bhṛgu; its metre is Gāyatrī; its deity is Pavamāna Soma. So the Anukramaṇī says: अया सोमः पंच कविर्भार्गवः (*ayā somaḥ paṃca kavirbhārgavaḥ*). The use in the rite has been stated.

Translation: This is the sūkta अया सोमः (*ayā somaḥ*). It is the twenty-third sūkta in the second anuvāka. It contains five ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kavi, the son of Bhṛgu; the deity is Pavamāna Soma; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: Kavi Bhārgava, five. The use in the rite has been stated before.

Sūkta 47. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 47. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 4. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Kavi Bhārgava. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒या सोमः॑ सुकृ॒त्यया॑ म॒हश्चि॑द॒भ्य॑वर्धत ।  
> मं॒दा॒न उद्वृ॑षायते ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अया । सोमः । सुऽकृत्यया । महः । चित् । अभि । अवर्धत ।  
> मंदानः । उत् । वृषऽयते ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 117

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोमः अया अनया सुकृत्यया शोभनयाभिषवादिलक्षणया क्रियया महश्चित् महतो देवान् प्रति अभ्यवर्धत प्रवृद्धोऽभूत् । मंदानः मोदमानः उद्वृषायते वृषवदाचरति । यथा मोदमानो वृषभः शब्दं करोति तथाभिषववेलायामुपरवेषु शब्दं करोतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Soma, by this good work (सुकृत्यया (*sukṛtyayā*)), that is, by an action of the kind of pressing and the like, grew mighty (महश्चित् (*mahaścit*)) in relation to the gods, that is, he became strong. Delighting (मंदानः (*maṃdānaḥ*)), he behaves like a bull (उद्वृषायते (*udvṛṣāyate*)). As a delighted bull makes a sound, so at the time of pressing he makes a sound on the pressing-stones.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the divine Soma
- अया सुकृत्यया (*ayā sukṛtyayā*)—by these excellent pressing and other (pressing out) rites
- महश्चित् (*mahaścit*)—even in the presence of the gods of exceedingly great power
- अभ्यवर्धत (*abhyavardhata*)—has gained increase
- मंदानः (*maṃdānaḥ*)—manifesting joy
- उद्वृषायते (*udvṛṣāyate*)—makes a sound, as a satisfied bull bellows

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The divine Soma, by these excellent pressing and other rites, has gained increase even in the presence of the gods of exceedingly great power. Manifesting joy, he makes a sound as a satisfied bull bellows.

##### English translation
By this sacred solemnity the Soma is magnified before the mighty
(gods): exulting he moves like a bull.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कृ॒तानीद॑स्य॒ कर्त्वा॒ चेतं॑ते दस्यु॒तर्ह॑णा ।  
> ऋ॒णा च॑ धृ॒ष्णुश्च॑यते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कृतानि । इत् । अस्य । कर्त्वा । चेतंते । दस्युऽतर्हणा ।  
> ऋणा । च । धृष्णुः । चयते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्य यस्य एतस्य सोमस्य दस्युतर्हणा दस्यूनामसुराणां नाशकानि कर्त्वा कर्माणि सः इत् अस्माभिरेव सोऽयं धृष्णुः धृष्टः सोमो यजमानानाम् ऋणा च ऋणान्यपि चयते कामप्रदानेन चातयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The deeds (कर्त्वा (*kartvā*)) of this Soma that destroy the Dasyus, the Asuras, are known. This bold Soma (धृष्णुः (*dhṛṣṇuḥ*)) also discharges (चयते (*cayate*)) the debts of the sacrificers, by granting their desires. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 118

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of this divine Soma
- दस्युतर्हणा (*dasyutarhaṇā*)—destroying the Asuras
- कर्त्वा (*kartvā*)—comprising the rites
- कृतानि इत् (*kṛtāni it*)—the heroic deeds themselves
- चेतंते (*cetaṃte*)—they proclaim
- धृष्णुः (*dhṛṣṇuḥ*)—Soma, the assailant of enemies
- ऋणा च (*ṛṇā ca*)—and the debts of the sacrificers
- चयते (*cayate*)—he discharges by granting what is desired

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The praisers proclaim the heroic deeds of this divine Soma, which comprise his Asura-destroying deeds. Soma, the assailant of enemies, also discharges the debts of the sacrificers by granting what is desired.

##### English translation
They publish his exploits, his acts of Dasya-crushing: resolute he
acquits the debts (of the worshipper).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आत्सोम॑ इंद्रि॒यो रसो॒ वज्रः॑ सहस्र॒सा भु॑वत् ।  
> उ॒क्थं यद॑स्य॒ जाय॑ते ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आत् । सोमः । इंद्रियः । रसः । वज्रः । सहस्रऽसाः । भुवत् ।  
> उक्थम् । यत् । अस्य । जायते ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत् यदा अस्य इंद्रस्य उक्थं शस्त्रं जायते प्रादुर्भवति तत् आत् अनंतरमेव इंद्रियः इंद्रस्य प्रियकरः रसः बलवान् वज्रः वज्रसदृशः केनाप्यहिंस्यः सोमः सहस्रसाः अस्मभ्यमपरिमितस्य धनस्य दाता भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When (यत् (*yat*)) the uktha (उक्थं (*ukthaṃ*)), the śastra of this Indra, arises, then immediately thereafter Soma, dear to Indra (इंद्रियः (*iṃdriyaḥ*)), the strong juice (रसः (*rasaḥ*)), like a thunderbolt (वज्रः (*vajraḥ*)) and not to be harmed by anyone, becomes the giver to us of boundless wealth (सहस्रसाः (*sahasrasāḥ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अस्य (*asya*)—addressed to Indra
- उकथं (*ukathaṃ*)—the śastra-mantra
- जायते (*jāyate*)—comes into being
- आत् (*āt*)—immediately thereupon
- इंद्रियः (*iṃdriyaḥ*)—dear to Indra
- वज्रः (*vajraḥ*)—the thunderbolt

### Printed p. 119

equal to the weapon of the thunderbolt;

- रसः (*rasaḥ*)—endowed with strength
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the Soma
- सहस्रसाः (*sahasrasāḥ*)—to us in thousands
- भवत् (*bhavat*)—becomes the bestower of wealth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the śastra-mantra addressed to Indra comes into being (is recited), immediately thereupon the Soma, dear to Indra, equal to the weapon of the thunderbolt and endowed with strength, becomes the bestower of wealth to us in thousands.

##### English translation
When the praise of Indra is recited, then the juice dear to him,
vigorous as a thunderbolt, gives us unlimited wealth.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒यं क॒विर्वि॑ध॒र्तरि॒ विप्रा॑य॒ रत्न॑मिच्छति ।  
> यदी॑ मर्मृ॒ज्यते॒ धियः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वयम् । कविः । विऽधर्तरि । विप्राय । रत्नम् । इच्छति ।  
> यदि । मर्मृज्यते । धियः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यदि कविः क्रांतकर्मा अयं सोमः धियः धीभिः। विभक्तिव्यत्ययः। धीतिभिः । अंगुलीभिरित्यर्थः । मर्मृज्यते शोध्यते तर्हि स्वयं स्वयमेव विप्राय मेधाविने विधर्तरि कामानां विधातरींद्रे रत्नं रमणीयं धनम् इच्छति । इंद्रेण धनं दापयितुमिच्छतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

If this Soma, the poet (कविः (*kaviḥ*)), that is, one of surpassing deeds, is cleansed by the धियः (*dhiyaḥ*), that is, by the fingers (with a change of case ending, for the instrumental), then of his own accord he desires wealth, a delightful treasure (रत्नं (*ratnaṃ*)), for the wise one (विप्राय (*viprāya*)) from Indra, the bestower (विधर्तरि (*vidhartari*)) of desires. The meaning is that he wishes Indra to give wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यदि (*yadi*)—when
- कविः (*kaviḥ*)—the divine Soma, of surpassing deeds
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—by the fingers
- मर्म्मृज्यते (*marmmṛjyate*)—is pressed for purification, then
- स्वयं (*svayaṃ*)—of his own accord
- विप्राय (*viprāya*)—for the wise
- विधर्तरि (*vidhartari*)—from Indra, the shower of desires
- रत्नं (*ratnaṃ*)—delightful wealth
- इच्छति (*icchati*)—he wishes to have given

### Printed p. 120

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the divine Soma, of surpassing deeds, is pressed by the fingers for purification, then of his own accord he wishes to have delightful wealth given from Indra, the wise one, the shower of desires.

##### English translation
When the sage Soma is cleansed by the fingers he of his own accord desires wealth for the pious worshipper in (Indra), the granter of wishes.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सि॒षा॒सतू॑ रयी॒णां वाजे॒ष्वर्व॑तामिव ।  
> भरे॑षु जि॒ग्युषा॑मसि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सिसासतुः । रयीणाम् । वाजेषु । अर्वताम्ऽइव ।  
> भरेषु । जिग्युषाम् । असि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम त्वं भरेषु संग्रामेषु जिग्युषां शत्रून् जयतां रयीणां धनानां सिषासतुः संभक्तुमिच्छुः असि भवसि । शत्रून् जयद्योत् धनानि प्रयच्छसीत्यर्थः । तत्र दृष्टांतः। वाजेषु संग्रामेषु अर्वतामिव अश्वानामिव । यथा संग्रामं प्रविशद्भ्योऽश्वेभ्यो घासं प्रयच्छंति तद्वदित्यर्थः॥ ॥ ४ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, you are one who wishes to share (सिषासतुः (*siṣāsatuḥ*)) among the wealth of those who conquer (जिग्युषां (*jigyuṣāṃ*)) enemies in battles (भरेषु (*bhareṣu*)). That is, you conquer enemies and bestow riches. An illustration is given: as in battles (वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)) people give fodder to the horses (अर्वतां (*arvatāṃ*)) that enter the battle, so it is here. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—entering battles
- अर्वतां इव (*arvatāṃ iva*)—as abundant food is given to horses
- भरेषु (*bhareṣu*)—in battles
- जिग्युषां (*jigyuṣāṃ*)—to heroes given to victory
- रयाणां (*rayāṇāṃ*)—abundant riches
- सिषासतुः असि (*siṣāsatuḥ asi*)—you wish to give

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, as abundant food is given to the horses that enter battles, so you wish to give abundant riches to the heroes who are victorious in battles.

### Printed p. 121

##### English translation
You desire to give wealth to those who conquer in combat as (men offer fodder) to horses in battle.

## Sūkta 48 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘ तं त्वा' इति पंचर्चं चतुर्विशं सूक्तम् । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । ‘तं त्वा' इत्यनुक्रांतम् । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning तं त्वा (*taṃ tvā*) has five ṛks and is the twenty-fourth sūkta. The seer and the rest are as before. The Anukramaṇī says तं त्वा (*taṃ tvā*). The use in the rite has been stated.

Translation: This sūkta तं त्वा (*taṃ tvā*) is the twenty-fourth sūkta in the second anuvāka. It contains five ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as of the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: तं त्वा (*taṃ tvā*). The use in the rite has been stated before.

Sūkta 48. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 48. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 5. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Kavi Bhārgava. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तं त्वा॑ नृ॒म्णानि॒ बिभ्र॑तं स॒धस्थे॑षु म॒हो दि॒वः ।  
> चारुं॑ सुकृ॒त्यये॑महे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तम् । त्वा । नृम्णानि । विभ्रतम् । सधऽस्थेषु । महः । दिवः ।  
> चारुम् । सुऽकृत्यया । ईमहे ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 122

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> महः दिवः महतो द्युलोकस्य सधस्थेषु सहस्थानेषु स्थितं नृम्णानि धनानि बिभ्रतम् अस्मदर्थं धारयंतं चारुं कल्याणं तं पवमानं त्वा त्वां सुकृत्यया शोभनया क्रियया ईमहे धनानि याचामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Pavamāna, you stand in the common abodes (सधस्थेषु (*sadhastheṣu*)) of the great heaven (महः दिवः (*mahaḥ divaḥ*)) and bear (बिभ्रतं (*bibhrataṃ*)) riches (नृम्णानि (*nṛmṇāni*)) for our sake. You are lovely (चारुं (*cāruṃ*)), that is, auspicious. We ask (ईमहे (*īmahe*)) you for riches by good action (सुकृत्यया (*sukṛtyayā*)), that is, by a good rite.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—of the vast
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—heaven
- सधस्थेषु (*sadhastheṣu*)—in the places of abode
- नृम्मानि (*nṛmmāni*)—riches
- भिभ्रतं (*bhibhrataṃ*)—who bear
- चारुं (*cāruṃ*)—charming
- तं (*taṃ*)—and purified
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- सुकृत्यया (*sukṛtyayā*)—by excellent rites
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we ask for those riches

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, you who bear the riches that lie in the abodes of the vast heaven, and are charming, we ask for those riches by excellent rites.

##### English translation
By sacred rites we solicit (wealth) of you, auspicious bearing wealth abiding in the regions of the vast heaven.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> संवृ॑क्तधृष्णुमु॒क्थ्यं॑ म॒हाम॑हिव्रतं॒ मद॑म् ।  
> श॒तं पुरो॑ रुरु॒क्षणि॑म् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संवृक्तऽधृष्णुम् । उक्थ्यम् । महाऽमहिव्रतम् । मदम् ।  
> शतम् । पुरः । रुरुक्षणिम् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम संवृक्तधृष्णुम् । संवृक्ताः संछिन्ना धृष्णवो धर्षणशीलाः शत्रवो येनासौ संवृक्तधृष्णुः। तम् उक्थ्यम् उक्थार्हं प्रशस्यं महामहिव्रतं महनीयबहुकर्माणं मदं मदकरं शतं बहूनि पुरः शत्रूणां पुराणि रुरुक्षणिं विनाशयंतं त्वां धनानीमह इति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, you are संवृक्तधृष्णुम् (*saṃvṛktadhṛṣṇum*): the one by whom the bold, assailing enemies are cut down. You are worthy of the uktha, to be praised (उक्थ्यम् (*ukthyam*)), of great and manifold deeds to be honoured (महामहिव्रतं (*mahāmahivrataṃ*)), exhilarating (मदं (*madaṃ*)), a destroyer (रुरुक्षणिं (*rurukṣaṇiṃ*)) of the hundred (शतं (*śataṃ*)), that is, many, cities (पुरः (*puraḥ*)) of enemies. The construction is: we ask you for riches.

### Printed p. 123

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- संवृक्तधृष्णुं (*saṃvṛktadhṛṣṇuṃ*)—you who cleave asunder the resisting, mighty enemies
- उक्ष्यं (*ukṣyaṃ*)—the praiseworthy
- महामहिव्रतं (*mahāmahivrataṃ*)—who has many venerable rites
- मदं (*madaṃ*)—the exhilarating
- शतं (*śataṃ*)—many
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—enemy cities
- रुरुक्षणिं (*rurukṣaṇiṃ*)—the destroyer; you we ask for riches

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, you who cleave asunder the resisting and mighty enemies, the praiseworthy, who have many venerable rites, the exhilarating, who destroy many cities of enemies, you we ask for riches.

##### English translation
The overthrower of the resolute (foe). the praiseworthy, to whom many
solemn observances are addressed, the exhilarator, the destroyer of a hundred
cities (of the Asuras).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अत॑स्त्वा र॒यिम॒भि राजा॑नं सुक्रतो दि॒वः ।  
> सु॒प॒र्णो अ॑व्य॒थिर्भ॑रत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अतः । त्वा । रयिम् । अभि । राजानम् । सुक्रतो इति सुऽक्रतो । दिवः ।  
> सुऽपर्णः । अव्यथिः । भरत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम रयिमभि धनं प्रति राजानं त्वा त्वाम् अतः दिवः अमुष्मात् द्युलोकात् सुक्रतुः सुप्रज्ञः अव्यथिः व्यथारहितः सुपर्णः श्येनः भरत् आहरत् । तथा च निगमांतरम्-- ‘ आदाय श्येनो अभरत्सोमं सहस्रं सवाँ' (ऋ. सं. ४.२६.७ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, from that heaven (दिवः (*divaḥ*)) the hawk (सुपर्णः (*suparṇaḥ*)), of good understanding (सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*)) and free from distress (अव्यथिः (*avyathiḥ*)), brought (भरत् (*bharat*)) you, who are king over wealth (रयिमभि (*rayimabhi*)). Another Vedic passage says the same: आदाय श्येनो अभरत्सोमं सहस्रं सवाँ (*ādāya śyeno abharatsomaṃ sahasraṃ savā~*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4.26.7) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 124

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- रयमभि (*rayamabhi*)—for the sake of wealth
- राजानं (*rājānaṃ*)—the lord
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अतः दिवः (*ataḥ divaḥ*)—from heaven
- सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*)—possessed of excellent deeds
- अव्यथिः (*avyathiḥ*)—and free from sorrow
- सुपर्णः (*suparṇaḥ*)—the hawk
- भरत् (*bharat*)—bore you and came

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, the hawk, possessed of excellent deeds and free from sorrow, bore you, the lord, from heaven for the sake of wealth, and came.

##### English translation
O (Soma), doer of good deeds, the unwearied hawk brought you, king over riches, from this heaven.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्व॑स्मा॒ इत्स्व॑र्दृ॒शे साधा॑रणं रज॒स्तुर॑म् ।  
> गो॒पामृ॒तस्य॒ विर्भ॑रत् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वस्मै । इत् । स्वः । दृशे । साधारणम् । रजःऽतुरम् ।  
> गोपाम् । ऋतस्य । विः । भरत् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रजस्तुरम् उदकस्य प्रेरकम् ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य गोपां गोपायितारं विश्वस्मै सर्वस्मै स्वदृशे सर्वदृशे देवाय साधारणम् इत् समानमेव संतं सोमं विः पक्षी श्येनः भरत् स्वर्गादाहरत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The bird (विः (*viḥ*)), the hawk, brought (भरत् (*bharat*)) from heaven the Soma who impels water (रजस्तुरम् (*rajasturam*)), who is the guardian (गोपां (*gopāṃ*)) of the sacrifice (ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)), and who is common (साधारणम् (*sādhāraṇam*)), that is, the same, for every god (विश्वस्मै (*viśvasmai*)) who sees all (स्वदृशे (*svadṛśe*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रजस्तुरं (*rajasturaṃ*)—the impeller of water in the form of rain
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the sacrifice
- गोपां (*gopāṃ*)—the protector
- विश्वस्मै (*viśvasmai*)—for all
- स्वर्दृशे (*svardṛśe*)—even for the gods of the world of the gods
- साधारणं इत् (*sādhāraṇaṃ it*)—equal, and worthy of reverence, Soma, the divine
- विः (*viḥ*)—the hawk
- भरत् (*bharat*)—bore and came

### Printed p. 125

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The hawk bore Soma, the divine, the impeller of water in the form of rain, the protector of the sacrifice, equally worthy of reverence to all the gods, and came.

##### English translation
The bird brought you, the shower of water, the protector of the sacrifice, the common property of every god.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधा॑ हिन्वा॒न इं॑द्रि॒यं ज्यायो॑ महि॒त्वमा॑नशे ।  
> अ॒भि॒ष्टि॒कृद्विच॑र्षणिः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । हिन्वानः । इंद्रियम् । ज्यायः । महिऽत्वम् । आनशे ।  
> अभिष्टिऽकृत् । विऽचर्षणिः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अध अथ विचर्षणिः कर्मणां विद्रष्टा अभिष्टिकृत् यजमानानामभीष्टस्य फलस्य कर्ता सोमः इंद्रियं स्वकीयं बलं हिन्वानः प्रेरयन् ज्यायः प्रशस्यतरं महित्वं महत्त्वम् आनशे प्राप्नोति ॥ ॥ ५ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Thereupon (अध (*adha*)) Soma, the one who sees (विचर्षणिः (*vicarṣaṇiḥ*)) all deeds, the maker (अभिष्टिकृत् (*abhiṣṭikṛt*)) of the desired fruit of the sacrificers, sending forth (हिन्वानः (*hinvānaḥ*)) his own strength (इंद्रियं (*iṃdriyaṃ*)), obtains (आनशे (*ānaśe*)) a more excellent (ज्यायः (*jyāyaḥ*)) greatness (महित्वं (*mahitvaṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—thereafter
- विचर्षणिः (*vicarṣaṇiḥ*)—able to look specially upon all affairs
- अभिष्टिकृत् (*abhiṣṭikṛt*)—accomplisher of the desired object of the sacrificer
- इंद्रियं (*iṃdriyaṃ*)—his own strength
- हिन्वानः (*hinvānaḥ*)—the divine Soma, who manifests
- ज्यायः (*jyāyaḥ*)—the most excellent
- महित्वं (*mahitvaṃ*)—greatness
- आनशे (*ānaśe*)—obtains

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

After being brought from heaven, the divine Soma, who is able to look specially upon all affairs, who accomplishes the desired object of the sacrificer, and who manifests his own strength, obtains his own most excellent greatness.

### Printed p. 126

##### English translation
The contemplator of all, the granter of desires, puttiug forth his
energy obtains superior greatness.

## Sūkta 49 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘पवस्व' इति पंचर्चं पंचविंशं सूक्तम् । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् ।  पवस्व' इत्यनुक्रांतम् । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning पवस्व (*pavasva*) has five ṛks and is the twenty-fifth sūkta. The seer and the rest are as before. The Anukramaṇī says पवस्व (*pavasva*). The use in the rite has been stated.

Translation: This sūkta पवस्व (*pavasva*) is the twenty-fifth sūkta in the second anuvāka. It contains five ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as of the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said पवस्व (*pavasva*). The use in the rite has been stated before.

Sūkta 49. Maṇḍala 9, Anuvāka 2, Sūkta 49. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 1, Varga 6. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Kavi Bhārgava. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पव॑स्व वृ॒ष्टिमा सु नो॒ऽपामू॒र्मिं दि॒वस्परि॑ ।  
> अ॒य॒क्ष्मा बृ॑ह॒तीरिषः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पवस्व । वृष्टिम् । आ । सु । नः । अपाम् । ऊर्मिम् । दिवः । परि ।  
> अयक्ष्माः । बृहतीः । इषः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 127

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम त्वं दिवः द्युलोकात् वृष्टिं नः अस्माकम् आ पवस्व समंतात् क्षर । एतदेव दर्शयति । अपाम् उदकानाम् ऊर्मिं तरंगं दिवः परि आ पवस्व । अपि च अयक्ष्माः यक्ष्मरहितानि अनामयानि बृहतीः महांति इषः अन्नानि आ पवस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, flow (आ पवस्व (*ā pavasva*)) for us on all sides rain (वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)) from heaven (दिवः (*divaḥ*)). He shows this very thing: flow from heaven the wave (ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*)), the billow, of the waters (अपाम् (*apām*)). Moreover flow free from disease (अयक्ष्माः (*ayakṣmāḥ*)), that is, healthy, great (बृहतीः (*bṛhatīḥ*)) foods (इषः (*iṣaḥ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from heaven
- वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—rain
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- आ पवस्व (*ā pavasva*)—pour all round
- सु (*su*)—in an excellent manner
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the waters
- ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*)—the waves
- परि (*pari*)—make them flow round about us
- अयक्ष्माः (*ayakṣmāḥ*)—free from disease
- बृहतीः (*bṛhatīḥ*)—and of great power
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—supply also foods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, pour rain for us from heaven everywhere. In an excellent manner make the flood of water in the form of waves flow. Supply also foods free from disease and of great power.

##### English translation
Pour down upon us a shower from heaven, a stream of waters, wholesome and abundant food.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तया॑ पवस्व॒ धार॑या॒ यया॒ गाव॑ इ॒हागम॑न् ।  
> जन्या॑स॒ उप॑ नो गृ॒हम् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तया । पवस्व । धारया । यया । गावः । इह । आऽगमन् ।  
> जन्यासः । उप । नः । गृहम् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम त्वं तया तादृश्या धारया पवस्व क्षर। कीदृश्येत्यत्राह। यया यादृश्या त्वदीयया धारया जन्यासः जन्याः शत्रुजनपदभवाः गावः इह अस्मिँल्लोके नः अस्माकं संबंधि गृहम् उप आगमन् उपागच्छंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, flow, that is, stream, in that stream of yours (तया (*tayā*) धारया (*dhārayā*)). He tells of what kind: by which stream of yours the cows (गावः (*gāvaḥ*)) that are जन्यासः (*janyāsaḥ*), that is, born in the country of the enemies, come (आगमन् (*āgaman*)) here, in this world, to our house (गृहम् (*gṛham*)).

### Printed p. 128

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O divine Soma)
- यया (*yayā*)—by which power of your flow
- जन्यासः (*janyāsaḥ*)—those among the enemy people
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—the cows
- इह (*iha*)—here in our region
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- गृहं (*gṛhaṃ*)—to the houses
- उप आगमन् (*upa āgaman*)—come and join
- तया (*tayā*)—by such
- धारया (*dhārayā*)—a stream
- पवस्व (*pavasva*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, pour in such a stream that by it, by the power of your flow, the cows that are among the enemy people come and join our houses in our regions.

##### English translation
Flow in such a stream that the cattle belonging to the (enemy's) nation may come here to our dwelling.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> घृ॒तं प॑वस्व॒ धार॑या य॒ज्ञेषु॑ देव॒वीत॑मः ।  
> अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ वृ॒ष्टिमा प॑व ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> घृतम् । पवस्व । धारया । यज्ञेषु । देवऽवीतमः ।  
> अस्मभ्यम् । वृष्टिम् । आ । पव ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम यज्ञेषु देववीतमः अत्यंतं देवकामस्त्वम् अस्मभ्यं भार्गवेभ्यः कविभ्यः घृतम् उदकम् । वनं घृतम्' इत्युदकनामसु पाठात् । धारया संपातेन पवस्व क्षर। वृष्टिं वर्षं च आ पव पवस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya
